  no `PublishedBy`, is *by you*.
- **A Codex lead's subagents** are bound only from the threads of their own calls (§1.1). For such a lead the group says that its
  subagents appear here only when they call Daoris, in place of *Nobody else is working on this*.

Each member row says **how it got there** (§3.4). It opens what it is: a part's page, a quest's page, a co-worker's thread (the
coworker design §6.4, R2-O13a) once COWORKUX1b has built it and the member's own session page until then (C5.3), a meeting's page
by its link (C6.1), or, for a native child, its console tab while the window holds it and its lineage facts after.

### 3.2 What waits on what (routing half)

Waits are edges, each from a record, never inferred from moments:

| Edge | Read from |
|---|---|
| the lead waits on its helper | `ended.awaits-helper` on the quest's current executor (§3.1); which helper is the exchange read's open ask whose asker is that part, read beside this one (§3.9, C6.1) |
| a taken quest waits on a quest it published | the quest unit's `awaits`, which is `Quest.Awaits`: **one quest at most** (`Quests.cs:96-102`), and **only while it stands** *(C2.1)*: its current executor is waiting (§3.1) and the question is not closed. The unit keeps the link after the answer and after the resume (`Planner.cs:38-50` at `972b17577`, SAYHELD1), so the link alone is never a wait |
| a part or a quest's session waits on the person, on a helper, on its own quest | that session's own state and its unit's `awaits`, the same edges one level down, under the same rule |
| a quest waits to be taken | its status `Open` (R2-O6: a unit's executors miss a chat's take and another machine's, `QuestUnits.cs:36-37`); *(C2.5)* the driver's tick's verdict and sentence for it (`sittingBecause`: not driven here, the repository busy, the cap spent, waiting for an account) are read on the page from the tick's own `considered`, beside the answer, never composed into it (§3.9) |
| a child runs under its parent | the lineage's `Parent`; an edge *inside*, not a wait |

The read answers the edges, and the view draws them as words on the waiting row: *waits on Codex · helper*, *waits on #a1b2 in
driver*, *not taken yet: the concurrency cap (3) is spent*. The session's own wait is drawn once at the top, first: **Waiting on** /
在等 *#a1b2 (driver lane)*. A session that published quests and waits on none says so: *published 3, waits on none*. That is the
truth for a chat that took no quest, which has no `Awaits`, and for a driven lead that handed rows on and finished (§6.2). The
subagents' group says how many run, *2 working*, and states no cause. *(R2-O4)* On the protocol door, where a child of the lead is
working, the heading adds *its turn waits for them*: the adapter holds the turn until the children it spawned end (§1.7). A waiting
part is live (§3.1), so a lead waiting on its own lane part is drawn as the lead with *Waiting on #q (core lane)*, and that part
below it. *(C2.1)* Once the question is answered the line goes; once the resume has begun, the resumed record is the lead and the
record that waited is folded among the earlier parts.

### 3.3 The channel's last words (routing half)

The view draws the ask's newest three channel messages, each with its moment and its author as the channel draws it, and places a
message on the member that said it by its session, which the page already carries and does not draw (`Channel.cs:87-104`).
*(C6.1)* The read carries no message: the lines are the ones the session's page already holds for its `ChannelPanel`
(`Web/work/AttendedSession.tsx:323-324`), and the ask's page for its channel section, so the view adds no reader and no wake for the
channel. *All messages* opens that panel. A session with no ask has no channel, and the block is not drawn.

### 3.4 The routing that put each member there (routing half)

Each member's row says the door that started it, read from its record:

| Member | Its routing, as the record says |
|---|---|
| a part opened by a lane quest | *published to the {lane} lane by part {n}* (`PublishedBy`, `Lanes`); *by you* only where the quest has no `PublishedBy`, `Parent` or ask |
| a part a chat took | *took #{quest}* (`TookQuest`) |
| a part opened by a sibling request of the same ask (`SessionEntries.cs:97`; D167 point 2) | *the ask's quest #{quest}* (R2-O7) |
| an earlier part | *handed on* with its `from_why`, or *retried*, as the parts strip says it |
| another repository's session | *asked of {repository} by part {n}*; *by ask #{id}* where `From` is an ask |
| a chain's step | *the next step of #{parent}* |
| a helper | *asked by this session* or *asked by you* (`askedBy`), under the rule `helpers` |
| a partner | *asked by you* (and by the lead once COAGENT1d lands) |
| a reviewer | *the workflow's second opinion* or *asked by you* (the opinion's occasion) |
| a native child | *started by {agent}*: the harness's own door, which Daoris neither starts nor confines (C2a held) |
| a brainstorm or meeting | *called by this session* or *by you*, once the request names it |

### 3.5 An ask's view (routing half)

An ask's page draws the same component over the ask. It shows the ask's quests, each with its lanes and its taking session, and each
of its entries with its parts, subagents and co-workers folded to a count that opens the entry's own view. It also shows the channel's
last words. It replaces nothing on the ask's page: `AskWork` and `AskLines` keep their presses. The section sits above them and links
into them.

### 3.6 What the records gain

Only two stored changes, both additive and both desktop-only:

1. **The lineage keeps a child's label, its parent, its end word and its moments, each process's start, and on each line the
   process that wrote it** (C2.2; C4-2.1).
   - **The protocol door.** The driver appends a line `by: "acp"` at the adapter's announcement, *(C4-3.2)* through a port
     `AcpStreams` is handed, so `AcpStreams` names no lineage type (§4.3), with:
     - the `subagentSessionId`;
     - the announced name and task, bounded: the name to 120 characters, the task's first line to 200;
     - **its place**: `parent`, the announcing wire session's id where that is a child's, or `depth: 1` where the root announced it.
       This is the one place on that door where a parent is said (`AcpStreams.cs:152-158`).
   - **The end.** It appends another `acp` line, with the wire's `state` word, **only from `subagent_state_update`**
     (`AcpStreams.cs:186-204`); *(O5-F3)* the word the wire said, and none where it said none, never `AcpStreams`' own default
     `ended` (`:189`), which is the console's. `EndAllAsync` writes no lineage line. A child still open when the process lets go
     is closed by the part's own `end` line, as `ended` where clean and `lost` where not. A killed root's child therefore reads *lost*, never
     *finished*.
   - **The pipe door.** The stream's start line keeps the task's `description`, bounded the same way. `ChildBindings.StreamLine`
     drops it today. Its end line already keeps the `status` (`ChildBindings.cs:313-318`).
   - **Every process is bracketed** *(C2.2, C2.3; made whole by C3-2.2, C3-2.3)*. Round 3 named two places; the driver starts a
     part's agent at four (§1.1): a chat's (`ChatRunner.cs:1441` at `b4f096cc0`), a driven session's first run (`Driver.cs:3643`), a
     continuation's run inside the same record (`DriverContinuations.cs:1242`) and a native resume's one retry (`:1299`). At each,
     the driver appends `{at, by: "start", proc, issuer}` *(C7-2.1: `issuer` below)* before the process is spawned *(O7-F1; round 7's "where its record may still
     refuse it, once the launcher has answered it instead" withdrawn: every `start` is written before its spawn, a record's start's
     too)*, *(O7-F4)* a run moved first writing it at its move (below), so it precedes every line that process's hook,
     stream or calls can write, and `{at, by: "end", proc, clean}` once that process's exit is read **and its capture has drained**
     *(O4-F4)*, clean as `End` judges it today (its exit code and no stop made). Draining is `CaptureWithinAsync` after the exit (`Driver.cs:3827`,
     then `:3833`; `DriverContinuations.cs:1272`, `:1274`; `:1312`, `:1314` at `deb766f20`), so a stream's `ended` line is written
     before the `end`, not after it; a chat's `end` is already written last, in its run's `finally` (`ChatRunner.cs:2854`). Where
     the drain's bound passes with the capture still open, the `end` is written at the bound, and *(C4-2.1)* a line the capture
     writes after it carries its own process's `proc`, which places it (below). The record's own conclusion writes no second `end`
     for a process already ended: `Driver.cs:3892`'s line (`:3907` at `eced2688e`) becomes the first run's own. *(C4-2.1)* **The
     `start` line makes the file** where there is none, so every line a process can write follows its `start`, the first
     process's included; its `end` goes to the file its `start` made, and `End` still writes to no file that does not exist
     (`ChildLineage.cs:118-122`). Round 4 wrote both only to a file that existed, which left a file's first process with no `start`.
     A part whose process started before row a has no file until a line of it creates one, as today.
     *(C5-1.1)* **A chat's `end` follows its capture on every path.** Where its advance to `working` throws before the capture is
     awaited (`ChatRunner.cs:2893`, `:2896`), the catch ends the process (`:2943`), and the run's `finally` *(O8-F3)* reads that
     process's exit, then waits for the capture within the drain's bound, before it writes the `end` (`:2977`), as a driven run's
     drain does; a line the capture writes past the bound carries its own `proc`, which places it (below). *(O8-F3)* **So no
     `end` is written before its process's exit is read**, on any path, a refused run's included (below): an `end` the fold reads
     is its issuer's word that the process has gone.
     *(C6-2.4, C6-3.1, O6-F1; round 6's void `end`, which O5-F1 named, withdrawn)* **A process is the record's only once the record
     has taken it.** Round 6 marked a process its record refused by a void `end` written once the refusal was handled. That left a
     window: from that process's `start` until its void `end` landed, the `start` closed the live generation, so a composition in
     between drew the live run's children *lost*, and a void `end` the file refused left them so for good. Round 7 marks the
     taking instead, so no `start` closes anything before its record is its process's:
     - **where the record is the process's before its spawn**, a resume moved first, a chat's go-on, a continuation's run and the
       retry (§1.1), its `start` says so, `{at, by: "start", proc, issuer, took: true}`, written before the spawn as round 6 wrote every
       `start`. *(O7-F4)* **A run moved first writes it at its move**: as soon as a resume's move to `working` returns moved
       (`Loop/DriverContinuations.cs:278-285`) and a go-on's returns (`Conversations/Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:667-674`, Ask Daoris's
       `:756-761`), before the preparation and the start gate the spawn still waits on (a resume's tree lock among them,
       `DriverContinuations.cs:301-304`), its token issued there and carried with the run to its spawn. A continuation's run
       and the retry, inside a record already `working`, make no move and write it before the spawn. A spawn that then fails,
       or a run moved and never spawned, leaves a taken `start` no line follows: it closed the generation before it, which its record had already moved on from, and its own generation, holding
       nothing, closes by rule 3 below *(O8-F3; round 7's "once the record goes back or fails" replaced)* once Loop has told its
       token that no process was spawned under it or the run was put back, which proves it never was;
     - *(O7-F5)* **a brainstorm's pass**, held with `movedFirst: true` and no record (§1.1), writes a taken `start` before its
       spawn and its `end` once its process lets go, as any run does. Nothing reads them: no entry holds a pass as a part, so no
       composition folds its file, and rule 3 never runs for it *(O8-F5: round 7's "which reads a record" dropped, rule 3 reading a
       token or an issuer now)*. Its `start` makes its folder's file at each
       pass, where today the connector's first call makes it, which every pass that answers makes, since a pass answers through the
       connector alone (`Loop/DriverBrainstorms.cs:665-667`);
     - **where the record takes the process only by an advance made after its spawn**, a record's start (§1.1), *(O7-F1; round 7's
       "once the launcher has answered its process … so a spawn that fails writes none" withdrawn)* its `start` is written before
       the spawn too, untaken, and `{at, by: "took", proc}` once the move to `working` is made (after `Driver.cs:3831`,
       `ChatRunner.cs:2893`). A launcher that answers no process (`Driver.cs:3661-3663`, `ChatRunner.cs:1596-1597`) leaves that
       `start` untaken and its generation empty, which rule 3 closes *(O8-F3; round 7's "once the record fails" replaced)* once
       Loop has told its token that no process was spawned under it, drawing nothing. Where the move is
       refused, the driven run writes its `end`, `{at, by: "end", proc, clean: false}`, at the refusal, *(O8-F3, read at
       `103fc1aa6`; round 7's "before `AbandonedAsync`", which wrote it before the kill, moved)* once `AbandonedAsync`'s
       `EndIfRunning` has ended its process (`Driver.cs:3838`, `:3971`) and that process's exit is read, before the capture's
       bounded wait (`:3972-3990`), and a chat's first process writes its own in its run's `finally` as above; neither `start` is
       ever taken. A line its capture writes after that `end` carries its `proc`, which
       places it in its own closed generation (below). *(O7-F1; round 7's "a line the process's hook or connector wrote before its
       `start` landed reads unplaced until it lands" withdrawn: true only of a line carrying a `proc`, and a line with none sitting
       before a new file's first `start` was history, placed by position, its generation closed `unrecorded` by the `took`.)* No
       line of the process precedes its `start`: a hook line the token missed, landing just after the spawn, sits after the
       file's first `start` and is unplaced, a count and never a state.

     The fold reads the whole file before it places a line, as it reads the roots (`ChildBindings.cs:128-163`), so a `start` is
     known taken wherever its `took` line sits, *(O8-F1)* and each taken generation's **taking** is known where it sits: its
     `start`'s place where that `start` says `took: true`, and its `took` line's place where a line took it. Rule 2 orders its
     closes by those places (below), never by the `start`s'. *(The Opus read's §3 item 4: round 6's "once `AbandonedAsync` has observed the
     capture" was wrong, since `AbandonedAsync` returns at its bound, observed or not, `Driver.cs:3956`, `:3983-3990`; the refused
     run's `end` no longer waits on it.)*
   - **Each line names its process** *(C4-2.1)*. Before each of the four spawns *(O7-F4: at the move, for a run moved first)* the
     driver issues a token, sixteen hex characters,
     shaped as an id (`ChildBindings.cs:85`; `lineage-hook.mjs:24`), and writes it as `proc` on that process's `start` and `end`
     and on every line the driver writes for it, the stream watcher's and the `acp` writer's. **The token is bound into that
     process's writers when they are made**: the watcher made for its capture (`HarnessCapture.cs:123`) and the sink handed to the
     `AcpStreams` of its connection (`Acp.cs:1668`) each hold it from the spawn, and none reads which process runs now. So a capture
     that outlives its process (§1.1) writes only as that process: that is the writer's fence. *(Round 6: the streams of a
     connection are made at `Acp.cs:1686` at `bdc1b03a2`.)*
   - **Who holds the token, and the `start`'s issuer** *(C7-2.1, read at `26ef34d80`; O8-F3, O8-F4 and C8-1.1, read at
     `103fc1aa6`)*. Rule 3 reads a generation's process by its token or its issuer (*how a generation's process is read*, below),
     so the token's process is told apart from its session's:
     - **The Daoris process that issues a token holds it**, in memory, *(O8-F3; round 8's "among its tokens in `SessionProcesses`"
       placed)* in **one set per Daoris process**, shared as `AgentJobs.Shared` is (`Platform/AgentJobs.cs:56`), never one per
       registry: a `SessionProcesses` is made per loop (`Modules/Loop/DriverLoop.cs:69`) and per loop context handed none
       (`Loop/LoopContext.cs:174`), and a set per registry would read another registry's live tokens of the same process as
       gone. A test hands its flow a set of its own, as `AgentJobs.Within` hands its jobs (`AgentJobs.cs:203-208`). A token is
       held from its issue, before the `start` is written and so before the spawn (at the move, for a run moved first).
       *(O8-F3)* **Loop hands it its process at the spawn**, beside `Track`, and the set watches that process's exit through a
       handle of its own, as `AgentStart.Lives` watches its process (`AgentJobs.cs:260-283`); the writer tells it its `end`'s
       write or refusal. **It leaves at the later of the two**, its process's exit seen and its `end` written or refused, and at
       nothing else but these: Loop tells it where its spawn made no process, or its run was put back before its spawn, and it
       leaves then; and Loop tells it where its run ended without writing its `end`, a throw past it or a closing loop
       (`Driver.cs:3910` is reached on the ordinary path alone, the reaper ending the process otherwise, `:3667`), and it leaves
       at its process's exit seen. So a token gone says its process has exited, or never was. Its leaving is told, since no line may say it.
       Each token is its own entry, so a second start of a record adds and removes only its own. `running` and the
       session's marker cannot say this: both are keyed by the session, a second start's `Track` replacing the first's entry and
       either's `Untrack` removing it (`Sessions/SessionProcesses.cs:408`, `:668`), and its marker written over and deleted alike
       (`:623-625`, `:640`), so once the run that took the record has ended, a process still held before its advance is in
       neither.
     - **Every `start` names that process**, `issuer: "{pid} {ticks}"`, its id and its start time as a session's marker keeps
       them (`SessionProcesses.cs:623`), read from the writing process before the spawn. *(O8-F4; round 8's "as a marker is read
       (`:493-519`), one that cannot be read counting as alive" corrected: `MarkerAlive` reads a marker whose fields do not parse
       as gone, `:505-508`, and its cannot-tell branches are `:500-503` and `:520-523`)* A reader that is not that process reads
       it by **one reading of a Daoris process's life, Platform's lease test** (`Platform/HomeLease.cs:426-449`, the test
       FOUNDATION1 Part B names for its process runs), made one Platform function that answers three ways: **alive**, a process of
       that id running under that start time, within a second; **gone**, no process of that id, or one of another start time; or
       **cannot be told**, its start time not this process's to read. The lease takes *cannot be told* as holding, as it does
       today (`:444-448`); rule 3 reads it as not known, never as alive and never as gone. An `issuer` that does not parse, and a
       `start` naming none, which no build writes, name an issuer that cannot be told.
     - *(C8-1.1; round 8's "every agent a Daoris process starts is held in a job that ends it with that process … so that process
       gone is its agents gone, but where the system made it no job, which rule 3's record half covers" withdrawn)* **An issuer
       gone says nothing of its agent.** Containment is best effort. A start the agents' job cannot hold is made as it was
       (`Platform/AgentJobs.cs:46-49`, `:127`), held then by the ORPHAN1 job its `Track` makes (`SessionProcesses.cs:405-406`),
       which can fail as well (`Platform/ProcessJob.cs:50`, `:53`, `:58`); and a start already the launcher's whose jobs do not
       hold this process's agents' job is uncontained and, the launcher's, held by no ORPHAN1 job (`AgentJobs.cs:111-117`). So
       an agent can outlive the Daoris process that started it. Whether a start was contained is decided inside the start
       (`Platform/AgentStarts.cs:33`), after its `start` is written, and is kept on no record a reader of the file holds: the
       machine log's `agent.contained` line names no session (`:40-55`). So no reader can tell a contained process from one that
       outlived its issuer, and rule 3 never reads an issuer gone as its agent gone.
   - **How the token reaches the hook and the connector** *(C5-2.1, O5-F4; round 5's "beside `DAORIS_SESSION_ID`
     (`Adapters.cs:1574`)" and "where their environment holds it" corrected, §1.1)*. They are the agent's own children, and each
     copies the token as `proc` where the variables it was handed hold one shaped as an id.
     - **The spawn's environment**: `DAORIS_LINEAGE_PROC`, set at each of the five places a start sets `DAORIS_SESSION_ID` by its
       target's kind (`Adapters.cs:1539`, `:1548`, `:1560`, `:1574`, `:1602`), *(O5-F9e)* so every kind of start carries it; set
       anew before each of the four spawns, since the retry reuses the first run's start whole *(the Opus read's §3 item 3; round
       6's "a continuation and a retry" corrected: a continuation's start is prepared anew from the same target, §1.1)*; and among the role
       variables a start drops when it inherits them (`Adapters.Roles`, `:1659-1665`, ENVLEAK1), so a driver run inside a session
       never writes as that session's process. *(O7-F6)* **Its name is declared once, in Platform**, beside the child marks
       `SessionVariable` and `RoleKeyVariable` (`Platform/ChildMarks.cs`, where FOUNDATION1's row mod-i moved the session marker's
       name, `IntakeRoom.SessionVariable` now reading it there, `Quests/IntakeRoom.cs:32`). Platform is the map's lowest subdomain,
       so `Adapters` (Agents) and the driver's writers name it downward, and no edge is added; never on `ChildLineage`
       (Participants) or beside `IntakeRoom` (Work orders), either of which `Adapters` would name upward. The hook and the
       connector, outside the driver, spell it as its twins, each with a test naming it (rule 21). The hook inherits its
       harness's environment, and on the pipe door so does Claude Code's connector, since Claude Code hands its servers its
       whole environment; whether the adapter hands the spawn's environment on to the harness it runs, and so to the hook under
       it, is rule 1's measure.
     - **The connector's entry, where it is composed per process**: the protocol door's, composed before each process opens its
       session and handed as that entry's `env` (`ChatRunner.cs:1548`, `Driver.cs:4315`; `Acp.cs:1651`, `:3423`, `:1757`). The token
       joins the entry's scope beside the session (`KnowledgeConnector.SessionScope`, `KnowledgeConnector.cs:114-124`). This is the
       one way it reaches Codex's connector, which is handed only its entry's own variables (`:95-96`, CONNECTOR2), so it is the
       one way a Codex child's call lines name their process. *(O6-F7; round 6's "every Codex child is known by its calls alone, so
       … a Codex child's lines" corrected.)* Where `codex-acp` announces a child, since the driver asks every streamed session for
       `nativeSubagentSessions` (`Acp.cs:1783-1790`; *the join*, below), its `acp` lines are the driver's own and carry the token
       bound into its writer. Codex starts a connector per thread from the same entry (`LineageFile.cs:24-26`), each so naming the
       process its thread runs in.
     - **Never the pipe door's server file.** A driven record's is written once and handed again to its continuation and its retry
       (`Driver.cs:2720`; `DriverContinuations.cs:1166`, `:1285-1286`), so a token in it would name the first run's process for
       every later one. The pipe door's one harness, Claude Code, hands its connector the spawn's environment instead.

     Whether the token reaches each writer on each door is still rule 1's measure, and where it does not, the line is *unplaced*
     (below): no line is given a process by a guess. *(Round 5's "a line that comes without one is placed by its position" is
     withdrawn, C5-2.1.)*
   - **Execution generations** *(C2.2, C2.3, C3-2.2, C3-2.3)*. The fold splits the lines into generations, one per process where
     the file recorded its processes. **Which generation a line is in** *(C4-2.1)*:
     - **a line with a `proc` is in the generation whose `start` names it**, wherever it sits in the file: a line its capture
       wrote late, after its process's `end` or after the next process's `start`, is still its own process's. *(C5-2.1; round 5's
       "placed as a line with none" withdrawn)* A `proc` no `start` names (its `start` lost, which `Append` never says,
       `ChildLineage.cs:108-111`) is **unplaced** (below);
     - **a line with no `proc` before the file's first `start`** (every line written before row a) is history, placed by its
       position: in the generation open where it sits. *(O4-F4)* **Where none is open, after an `end`, it begins a generation**, as
       today's fold reads an `end` as the boundary between runs (`ChildBindings.cs:176-177`), and the owner's case is such a file.
       One exception: a line that only ends a run of the generation that `end` closed (a stream's `ended`, an `acp` end, a hook's
       `stopped`, naming an id with a run there), coming before any line of a later generation, stays in that generation, its end
       word taken as the end-word rule below has it, and begins nothing;
     - *(C5-2.1, C5-3.1; round 5's "placed by its position" withdrawn for these)* **a line with no `proc` from the file's first
       `start` on is unplaced**: the hook's or the connector's where the token did not reach them, or a line an older build wrote
       after a rollback. After a `start` a line's position says nothing of its process: an old stop sitting after P2's `start` may
       be P1's, and an old start there may be.

     ***Unplaced*** *(C5-2.1, C5-3.1)*: a line attributed to no process, because it carries no usable token or its token's `start`
     is missing. It is evidence that its agent was named, and nothing more, so it is never given a state of a definite generation:
     it begins, ends and words no run, opens and closes no generation, and moves no *last seen*. Each agent id unplaced lines name
     is counted once, `unplaced`, beside the group: *(O6-F8; round 6's words, which named the lineage file, reworded, §5.4)*
     *{{count}} agent ids it named could not be tied to a process*.
     Like the hook's unmatched ids (*the join*, below), they are never children: never rows or runs, never in *n working* or a
     part's subagents' count. An id among them may also be a row's, since the count says only that some lines naming it could not
     be tied; and where unplaced and unmatched ids are all a part holds, their counts stand in the empty sentence's place (§5.2).
     So an old tokenless stop after P2's `start` never closes P2's reused X, and an old tokenless start never makes a working child
     there: D173's partial visibility, said rather than filled (ADR-B, `D173.md:11-12`). Rule 1 measures how many lines each door
     leaves unplaced, so the residue is a count; it changes no state.

     **The first pass reads every line for who an id is, never for which process wrote it** *(O6-F2)*. Before it places a line,
     the fold reads the whole file in no order (`ChildBindings.cs:128-163`): which harness the part runs, its root (Claude Code's
     session, Codex's threads named with no parent and the root its metadata names), Claude Code's join of a call's `toolUse` to
     the agent the hook names for it, and, from round 7, which `start`s are taken. That pass reads every line, unplaced ones
     included, and what it learns is identity alone; a line's placement and the state it moves follow its own `proc`, as above. So
     a connector call carrying its process's `proc`, whose `toolUse` only an unplaced hook line joins to agent X, is X's call,
     placed by its own `proc`: on a part with no `acp` lines it begins X's *first seen* run in its generation, as any call naming
     an id with no run there does, and on a part with `acp` lines X is counted among the unmatched ids (*the join*). The hook line
     itself counts X once in `unplaced` and moves nothing.

     **Recorded and unrecorded boundaries** *(C4-1.1)*. A generation a `start` began is *recorded*: its process's boundaries are
     its `start` and its `end`. A generation begun without one is *history*: before row a no process wrote a `start`, a chat wrote
     one `end` per process, and a driven record one for all of its processes (§1.1). So a history generation's own `end` is its one
     process's only where the record is a chat, by its `kind`, which the `sessions` slice already holds (`SessionsStateSlice.cs:41`;
     `kind?: 'driven' | 'chat'`, `Web/data/api.ts:445`); *(O5-F9a)* a record with no `kind`, which the type allows, is read as
     driven, the reading that claims no boundary. In a driven record's history generation the boundaries between its
     processes were never written, and the fold claims none: a run there that no harness ended may have ended at any of them, so
     it reads **`unrecorded`**, *its end was not recorded · last seen {time}*, never `ended`, `lost` or the `end`'s moment, which may
     be a later process's. *(C5-2.1; round 5's history generation begun after a `start` by a line with no `proc` withdrawn)* A
     history generation sits only before a file's first `start`: from that `start` on, every generation is recorded, and a line
     with no `proc` there is unplaced. A generation closes at the first of *(O7-F7: a recorded generation's own `end` before
     either of the others, wherever it sits)*:
     1. its own `end` line, `ended` where clean and `lost` where not, as today (`ChildBindings.cs:241-244`), at the line's moment:
        a recorded generation, and a chat's history one; *(C4-1.1)* a driven record's history generation closes here too, its runs
        no harness ended reading `unrecorded`. *(O7-F7; round 7's "first of" in the file's order narrowed for it)* **A recorded
        generation's own `end` closes it wherever it sits in the file.** It names its `proc`, and the fold reads the whole file
        before it places a line, so where the file holds that `end` it is the generation's close, whatever `start` sits before it,
        and the second and third ways close only a generation whose `end` the file does not hold. A chat concludes its record
        before its run's `finally` writes its `end` (`ChatRunner.cs:2935`, `:2954`, `:2963`, `:2967`, then `:2977`), and a driven
        run's `end` follows its conclusion too (`Driver.cs:3894-3906`, then `:3910`), so a go-on's or a resume's taken `start`,
        which needs the record ended, may land between them: that generation reads `lost` by the second way only until its `end`
        lands, and as its `end` says from then (§9);
     2. *(O8-F1, C8-2.3; round 8's "the next `start` line that its record took", which closed a generation taken by a late `took`
        at a taken `start` written before that `took`, replaced, read at `103fc1aa6`)* **the first taking after its own, in the
        file's order**. A *taking* is a `start` with `took: true`, at that `start`'s place, or a `took` line, at its own place. A
        taken generation's own taking is the one that took it, so a generation a `took` line took is taken where that line sits,
        whatever `start` sits between its `start` and it; a history generation's place is where it sits, before the file's first
        `start`. Takings sit in the order the record takes its runs. Each is written by its issuer as its run takes the record (a
        `took` once the advance returns, after `Driver.cs:3831` and `ChatRunner.cs:2893`; a run moved first's `start` as its move
        returns), before that run can let the record go. The record is taken again only once the run that held it has let it go,
        at its conclusion after its process's exit, at a stop after its kill, or by the orphan sweep once the process its
        session's marker names is gone; a continuation's run and the retry take a record already `working`, written by the same
        issuer after the earlier process's exit (`DriverContinuations.cs:1242`, `:1299`). **So a later taking proves that the
        process the taking before it took has exited**, and closes that generation where its `end` is missing: `lost` for a
        recorded generation, whose process went without its `end`, which a crash of the application or the driver between the
        process's exit and its `end` line leaves, or a write of that `end` the file refused (`ChildLineage.cs:108-111`); *(O5-F1;
        round 5's "which only a crash … leaves" corrected)* a duplicate's `start` once left one too, beside a live process, and
        closes nothing now, never taken; *(C4-1.1)* `unrecorded` for a history one, whose process went before anything wrote its
        end (the first taking after an upgrade closes a record's history so). *(C6-2.4)* It closes no untaken generation,
        *(C7-2.1; round 7's named changes' "but where it was taken before its spawn, `took: true`" withdrawn)* however it was
        taken (below). The one way a taking does not follow its predecessor's exit is a record its session's registry let the
        orphan sweep stop while the run that took it lived, which §9 names (O8-F5);
     3. for a generation neither its own `end` nor a later taking closed *(C6-2.4; round 6's "for the last generation only, the
        last that is not void" replaced)*, which is the last taken one, and *(C7-2.1)* any untaken one: *(C8-1.1, C8-2.2, C8-3.1,
        O8-F2, O8-F5; round 8's "the part's process being gone: no process of the part runs in this window (the driver slice's
        `running`) and its record is neither `queued`, `starting` nor `working`" for a taken one, and its two predicates for an
        untaken one, replaced, read at `103fc1aa6`)* **its process's exit proven**, read by its `start`'s `issuer` as *how a
        generation's process is read* (below) has it; **where its process is read neither running nor gone, its open runs read
        not known**, `uncertain`, the generation left open. A recorded generation it closes reads `lost` where it is untaken, since its record says nothing of
        it, and where it is taken, by its record's state: `lost` where the record is `stopped` or `failed`, or has not left
        `working`, and `ended` where it is `completed`, `awaiting-person`, `declined` or `stood-down`, each a clean end (O4-F7;
        the nine states, `Web/data/api.ts:413-415`). *(C3-2.3)* This reads the process, never responsibility: a record `completed`
        with a wait code is closed here as any other, so the crash between `Driver.cs:3876`'s conclusion and the `end` line leaves
        no child working. A crash's taken generation, its issuer gone and its record still `working`, reads not known until the
        orphan sweep moves the record `stopped` as the driver's watch comes up (`Orphans.cs:74-81`, once per watch start,
        `DriverWatch.cs:715-717`), a move that wakes the read (§4.1) and closes it. *(C4-1.1; O8-F5)* A history generation names no
        process and no issuer, so it is read as one whose issuer cannot be told: closed where its record is neither `queued`,
        `starting` nor `working`, its runs no harness ended in a driven record's history reading `unrecorded` whatever the
        record's state, and not known otherwise. *(O4-F6, O6-F1; O8-F5: rounds 4 to 8's reading by `running`, this window's
        processes keyed by the session, withdrawn)* No generation is read by `running`. A second start's `Untrack` leaves
        `running` without the live run (§1.1), which is why round 6 read the record beside it; a token or an issuer is the
        process's own.

     **An untaken process** *(C6-2.4, O6-F1; round 6's void process replaced)*. A generation whose `start` its record never took
     holds its own process's lines alone, placed by its `proc`, and it closes no generation. *(C7-2.1, C7-3.1)* **It closes only at
     its own `end` or by rule 3's proof that its own process has exited**, never by a later taking, never by its record's move
     *(C8-1.1; round 8's record half withdrawn: its record was never its process's, so the record's move proves nothing of it)*,
     and never by its issuer's exit alone *(C8-2.2)*. Its own `end` closes it as any recorded generation's does: a refused run's
     `end` is unclean, so an open run there reads `lost`.

     **How a generation's process is read** *(C8-1.1, C8-2.2, C8-3.1, O8-F2, O8-F4, O8-F5, read at `103fc1aa6`; round 8's two
     predicates for an untaken generation, "absent from this machine's processes" and "its record not running it", and rules 4 to 8's
     `running` and record for a taken one, replaced)*. Rule 3 reads the process of a recorded generation that no `end` and no
     later taking closed by its `start`'s `issuer`, by the evidence the reader holds, and closes it only where that proves the
     process has exited:
     - **the reading Daoris process issued it** (the `issuer` names it, its id and its start time within a second): **by its
       token alone**. Held, its runs read as their harness says, *working* or `unsaid`. Gone, the generation closes, whatever its
       record says: the token leaves only once that process's exit has been seen and its `end` written, refused or, its run
       ending without one, forgone, or where no process was spawned under it or its run was put back before its spawn (*who
       holds the token*, above), so its exit, or its never being, is proven. *(O8-F2a)* Round 8 read the record beside it, which added nothing a token seen to leave does not
       prove, and kept a refused run's ended children open where the run that took the record had taken it before the refused
       run's `start`. *(O8-F5)* Nor is `running` read for a taken one, so a second start's `Untrack` leaving `running` without
       the live run changes nothing here;
     - **another Daoris process issued it, and is alive**: its runs read as their harness says, **whatever its record says**.
       That process holds its token and writes its `end`, which closes it, and its record's move speaks for the process only once
       its issuer can no longer. *(O8-F5)* So a record another Daoris process's sweep stopped while the run it took still lives,
       which the session's registry allows (§9), closes nothing while that run's issuer lives;
     - **another Daoris process issued it, and is gone or cannot be told**, or its `start` names an issuer that cannot be told:
       - **a taken generation whose record is neither `queued`, `starting` nor `working` closes**, by its record's word (above).
         The record left `working` only once the process it took had exited (its conclusion after the exit, a stop after the
         kill, the orphan sweep only once the process its session's marker names is gone, `Trees/Orphans.cs:74-76`,
         `Sessions/SessionProcesses.cs:455-459`), and its issuer can write no `end` now;
       - **otherwise its open runs read not known** *(C8-1.1, C8-2.2, C8-3.1)*: in every untaken generation, whose record says
         nothing of its process, and in a taken one whose record is still `queued`, `starting` or `working`. Its issuer's exit
         proves nothing of its agent (*who holds the token*, above), and that issuer can no longer write its `end`. Such a run
         is **`uncertain`**, *not known whether it still runs · last seen {time}*: never *working*, since nothing that runs holds
         its token, and never `lost`, since nothing proves its process went. The generation is not closed: a line of its process
         placed after that is placed and moves its runs as any line does, and it closes once a proof comes, a later taking for a
         taken one, its record's move past `working`, or its own `end`, which nothing writes once its issuer has gone, so an
         untaken one stays not known for good (§9).

     *(C7-2.1, C8-2.2, C8-2.3, O8-F1)* Round 7's named changes closed an untaken generation at a `start` taken before its spawn,
     and round 8 by its issuer's exit or its record's move. None of these says that a competitor has gone. A record's start
     awaits the service after its spawn (`Driver.cs:3673`, `:3681`) and again at its advance (`:3831`); while it waits, the run
     that took the record can finish and its continuation spawn (`DriverContinuations.cs:1242`), or its record conclude and its
     next run be moved, and the Daoris process that started it can go while an agent it could not contain runs on. So where two
     starts of one record overlap, each generation reads as its own process's lines say until its own `end` or a proof of its
     process's exit. The run that took the record is read as any taken run. The refused one's children read as their harness
     says from its `start`, through the taking run's end, its continuation and its record's later moves while the refused one
     waits before its advance, until its `end` at the refusal, written once its process's exit is read (O8-F3), its later lines
     placed in it by its own `proc`. Where the refused one's issuer is another Daoris process that goes before that `end`, its
     children read not known from that exit, never *lost* (C8-2.2). A competitor its record admits late, as a go-on of the
     concluded record (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1723-1726`), is taken at its `took`, the last taking in the file, so no
     taking closes it while it runs (O8-F1, C8-2.3). A composition held at any point between reads the same as a fresh
     composition of the same lines, tokens, issuers and records, since each of them wakes the read as it moves (§4.1). A `start`
     whose advance is not made yet is untaken too: until its `took` lands it is read by its own lines and its process, and the
     generation before it, where one is open, is untouched. A `took` line or a refused run's `end` the file refused leaves its own
     generation to rule 3, never a live one closed (§9).

     **Rules 2 and 3 are Daoris's reading, not an end anyone said** *(O3-F11e)*. A child they close carries no end moment: it reads
     *lost* or *ended with its part* with *last seen {time}*, its own last line's moment in that generation, never the sweep's
     `updated` or the next start's. *(C4-1.1)* So is `unrecorded`, wherever it is read: *its end was not recorded · last seen
     {time}*; *(C8-1.1)* and so is `uncertain`, which closes nothing: *not known whether it still runs · last seen {time}*. The read
     passes the record's state and its `kind` to the fold, *(C7-2.1, O8-F4, O8-F5; round 4's running set no longer passed)* and
     for each recorded generation whether this Daoris process holds its token, where it issued it, or else its issuer's answer,
     alive, gone or cannot be told; nothing is written.

     ***Last seen* moves only while its run is open** *(C5-2.2, O5-F9c)*. A run's *last seen* is the moment of its last line
     before the line that closed it, in the file's order: its generation's `end` *(O7-F7: wherever it sits)*, *(C6-2.4)* where the
     file holds none the taking that closed it *(O8-F1: a taken `start` or a `took` line)*, or, for a run begun in a generation already closed (below), its own first
     line. A line of the run's process placed after that, a late
     call or a stop that begins nothing, changes no run, so it moves nothing the answer carries, *last seen* included: the call
     line that wakes nothing (§4.1) moves nothing either, and a fresh composition draws what the held one does. A run rule 3
     closed has no line that closed it, since rule 3 reads the process gone: its *last seen* is its generation's last line naming
     it, and the composition registers it so that a call naming it wakes the read (§4.1), the one call that moves an answer. Rule
     3 closes a generation *(C6-2.4; round 6's "a last generation")* *(C8-1.1; round 8's "only once its part's process is gone and
     its record has moved past `working`, an untaken one only once its own process is gone and its record past it" replaced)* only
     once its process's exit is proven, so a call naming one of its runs is rare, and waking on it costs little. *(C8-1.1)* **A
     run read not known** has no line that closed it either, and is not closed: its *last seen* is its last line in its
     generation, moved by each later line of its run, and the composition registers it as it registers a run rule 3 closed, so a
     call naming it wakes the read and moves its *last seen*. Such calls come only from an agent outliving the Daoris process
     that started it, the case the state is for.

     A child's state and moments belong to **its run** *(C2.3; made whole by O3-F1, C3-2.1)*:
     - a run begins at a start the harness said (the hook's `started`, the stream's `started`, an `acp` announcement), from that
       moment;
     - **or, for a native id with no run in this generation, at its first line of any kind, a call's included**: a child no harness
       said the start of, as every Codex child is (§1.1), is kept, its start unknown, drawn *first seen {time}* from that line's
       moment;
     - it ends at a stop its harness said (the hook's `stopped`, the stream's `ended`, an `acp` end) or at its generation's close;
     - a start the harness said after a stop, in this generation or a later one, begins a new run: *working*, from its new start;
     - **a call or a stop line naming a native id whose run in this generation already ended begins nothing**, so a late call
       never reopens a finished child; a call naming it in a later generation, with no said start there, begins that
       generation's run as above.
     - *(O4-F1)* **A run whose harness says no end is never *working*.** Codex says no thread's start or end (§1.1), so its run
       stays open until its generation closes, which on `codex-acp`, one process across a chat's turns (`Adapters.cs:2063-2067`),
       is the chat's whole process. While open, such a run's state is `unsaid`: *first seen {time} · its end is not said*. It is
       counted as working nowhere: not in *n working*, not in a part's subagents' working count, not in the head's *working with
       it*. Its generation's close ends it as any other (*ended with its part*, *lost*).
     - *(C8-1.1, C8-2.2)* **A run open in a generation rule 3 reads not known is `uncertain`**, whether its harness had it
       *working* or `unsaid`: *not known whether it still runs · last seen {time}*, counted as working nowhere, like `unsaid`. A
       run its harness ended there keeps its word. Its generation's close, once a proof comes, ends it as any other.
     - *(O4-F8)* **The end word.** Where a line of the run says a word, the wire's (`acp`) or the stream's, that word is the run's
       end, whichever line comes first; otherwise the hook's stop makes it `finished`. A hook `stopped` and a wire's
       `subagent_state_update` thus end the same run with the wire's word, never the order of their lines.
     - *(C4-2.1)* **A line placed in a generation already closed** (a line its capture wrote late, carrying its process's `proc`)
       changes that generation alone. An end it says ends or words its run there, as the end-word rule has it. A start it says
       begins a run there that the generation's close has already ended: the run reads as that close has it, with *last seen* at
       its own line, never *working*. It begins no generation and touches no run of a later one. So a late start never makes a
       generation, and an old stop never ends the later run of a reused id.

     The earlier runs of a native id stay in the fold as history and are never drawn as second rows. This replaces `Of`'s clearing
     of `Closed` alone (`ChildBindings.cs:177`), under which a stopped child's `Stopped` won for good (`:278`). *(O4-F4)* That
     clearing was also how today's fold reads an `end` as a boundary between runs; the generation rule above keeps that boundary.
   - **Unreadable is not empty** *(C4.1)*. `ChildLineage.Read` answers three outcomes: no file (nothing recorded), read, and
     unreadable, with the error's kind, where today every `IOException`, a missing file's included, folds as empty
     (`ChildLineage.cs:135-138`). An unreadable lineage is drawn as *its subagents could not be read* on that part, never as none,
     and keeps the view's empty sentence away. A missing file is a dependency of the read stamped absent (§4), since the first
     child's line is what creates it, or the root's first call (O4-F12e), or from row a on its first process's `start` (C4-2.1).
   - **A teammate's part is not read here** *(O5-F6)*. An entry holds every record of its key, a teammate's among them: the
     entries are grouped with no teammate filter (`Driver/Trees/SessionEntries.cs:96-98`). A teammate's record is one that came
     down from the team, its id `origin/id` (`Driver/Trees/SessionGroups.cs:132`), and its process ran on another machine, so no
     lineage of it is here, and the lineage's session shape refuses its id (`ChildLineage.cs:44`, `:127`). Such a part's
     subagents read *its subagents are not visible on this machine*, never a count and never none: no file is looked for, and the
     empty sentence is withheld, as it is for an unreadable lineage. The view draws the sentence on the part's row, and in the
     *Subagents* group where the attended part is one.
   - **The hook** stays ids only. A label is not an id, and the hook's rule (`lineage-hook.mjs:10-12`) is that no value a path may
     hide in reaches the file through it.
   - **The fold** keeps, per run:
     - each child's first and last `at`, and whether its start was said or only first seen (O3-F1); the read's answer carries the
       first, the end the harness said with its moment, and, for a run rules 2 or 3 closed *(C8-1.1)* or read `uncertain`, the
       last as *last seen*, never a working run's last, which only call lines would move (O3-F5, §4); *(C5-2.2)* that last is its
       last line before the line that closed it, *(C8-1.1)* or an `uncertain` run's last line, as *last seen moves only while its
       run is open* (above) has it;
     - its label, where a line gave one;
     - its parent and depth from an `acp` line, beside the stream's and Codex's;
     - **the end word as the wire or the stream said it**: `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `disconnected`, *(O5-F3)* and
       `stopped`: the stream keeps any id-shaped `status` (`ChildBindings.cs:316`), and `stopped` is one of its words for a
       stopped task (`Driver.Tests/fixtures/native/claude-background-cut.stream.jsonl:14`, a background command's, the same
       `task_notification` an agent's end is), which the panel already words
       (`work.panel.stream.state.stopped`, `Web/locales/en/work.panel.json:18`). A stop with no word (the hook's, or an `acp` end
       whose wire said none) stays `finished`, and *(O5-F3)* so does a word none of these five: an end said with an outcome the view
       cannot word, never a state of its own and never the fold's own `ended`. The view draws each with the output panel's own words
       (`work.panel.stream.state.*`), adding `disconnected`, and *(C4-3.3)* every other state, `finished` and `unrecorded` among
       them, with the key §5.4's mapping names.
   - **Why a label may enter a file the rule kept to ids.**
     - The file is the session's, under the home, never synced and never on a shared host (D47).
     - For a child of the root, the label is already on the session's events as the card's title and text (`AcpStreams.cs:169-181`).
     - For a nested child, this is the first time its name and task reach disk (they were held only in memory, §1.1). It is the same
       class of text, from the same structured field of the harness's wire, kept in the same session's own folder. It is never parsed
       from the conversation, and it is bounded.
     - *(R2-O13f)* A task's first line may name a path. The lineage's twin row (`.claude/knowledge/twins.md:180`) says a value is
       kept only where it is shaped as an id, *so no path is written*, which the label ends. Row a amends that row, adding the
       driver's `acp` and `start` lines beside its `stream` and `end` (C2.2), and *(C4-2.1)* `proc` on each line, the hook's and
       the connector's where their environment holds it, *(O5-F4)* the connector's from its entry's variables, *(C6-2.4, O6-F1;
       round 6's `void` on an `end` withdrawn)* and the taking,
       `took` on a `start` and a `took` line of its own, *(C7-2.1)* and `issuer` on a `start`, and D173's note says the file
       becomes transcript-class, kept on this machine (D47).
   - **The join.** The CONSOLE2 evidence says what the ids are: `subagentSessionId` is the harness's agent id, and a re-run is
     `<id>:generation:<n>` (§1.1). The hook's `agent_id` is the same harness's agent id.
     - The fold joins an `acp` child to a hook child where the ids are equal, or where the `acp` id is the hook's id followed by
       `:generation:<n>`. That is the same child run again: a new run of it, as above.
     - **An `acp` child that joins nothing stays the child's one row.** Hook starts that join no `acp` child on a part that has
       `acp` lines are not drawn as rows. *(C4.1, replacing R2-O8's two halves; counted by id since round 4.)* They are kept as
       what they are, **agent ids the hook named that no announcement matched**, and said as a count beside the group: *its hook
       also named 2 agent ids it could not match*. They are never counted as children: not in *n working*, not in a part's
       subagents' count. *(O3-F9, C3-4.1;
       round 3's "never a reason for the empty sentence to be withheld" withdrawn, since it contradicted the next point.)* **Where a
       part's subagents hold only such ids, the empty sentence is not drawn: the count stands in its place**, with its story, since
       evidence whose end nobody said establishes no absence. The generation suffix is the adapter source's word, not a measurement
       (CONSOLE2 evidence `:61-62`), and whether a re-run's hook `agent_id` is the same id or a new one is unknown, so the count says
       neither that the agent did not announce a child nor that these are other children.
     - *(Round 4, with O3-F1.)* **What is counted is agent ids, not starts.** On a part with `acp` lines, every hook line of an
       agent id no announcement matched, its start, its stop or its calls, is evidence of that one id: counted once, never a row,
       and never a run (the first-line rule above draws rows only from the harness's children the part's rows come from: Codex's
       threads, and on a part with no `acp` lines, Claude Code's agents). Whether `SubagentStart` reaches the hook over the adapter
       at all is row a's first measure (§1.1), so an id the hook names only by a call must be counted too. The words say ids:
       *its hook also named 2 agent ids it could not match*.
     - *(O4-F1)* **A Codex part draws its rows from one source.** The driver asks every streamed protocol session for
       `nativeSubagentSessions` (`Acp.cs:1756-1770`), so `codex-acp` may announce children as `acp` lines as well as its calls
       naming threads, and nothing joins the two today. Row a's measure (rule 1) records whether `codex-acp` announces children and
       whether its announced ids equal the threads its calls carry. Until D173's note records it, a Codex part with `acp` lines draws
       its rows from them, with their ends, and counts each thread no announcement matched once, as the hook's unmatched ids are
       counted; a Codex part with none draws its rows from its threads. Where the measure finds the ids equal, they join as the
       hook's do.
     - **Where row a's measure finds that a door's ids never join the hook's**, the count stands as worded, since it claims no
       child, and D173's note records the measure. Round 2 hid them there; a door whose evidence the view hid could draw *Nobody
       else is working on this* over a hook that saw starts, which D173's ADR-B forbids (`D173.md:11-12`).
     - On a part with no `acp` lines (the pipe door), the hook's and the stream's children are the rows, as they join today by
       `task_id`.
     - Nothing joins by time or by order.
     - Row a measures the join on the install's lineage files and on a stand-in's, on a first run and on a re-run, before the fold
       relies on it. Its counts go in D173's note.
2. **A brainstorm's and a meeting's request name who called it**: `session` and `ask`, null where the person did. This is additive,
   written by the door that makes the request. It is row f's, after BRAINSTORM1d and AGENTROOM1b give a session a door to call one.

Nothing else is stored: no team record, no structure, no lane on the session record (a part's lane is its quest's), no stored wait,
no turn. One fact is kept in memory and not stored: each console stream's newest moment, for *last heard* (§3.7).

### 3.7 The record's `updated`, and *last heard*

`updated` keeps its meaning, *when the record last moved*. Advancing it on every event would make *ran* and *waiting* wrong
(`headFacts.ts:169`, `:175`, `:207`), and would write the service's store at the rate of the agent's events.

On the protocol door, a child's words and tools never reach the session's events, and its lineage gets a line only at its start, its
end and its connector calls (§1.1). A moment taken from the events and the lineage would read *38 minutes ago* for a child that has
worked for 38 minutes. So each working part has a **last heard**, the first of these that holds:

1. **What the window heard**: the newest line of the part's own console stream and its children's streams. *(R2-O13d)*
   `SessionOutput` keeps no moment today (`ConsoleLine(Sequence, Text)`, `SessionOutput.cs:9`; `Buffer`, `:98-112`). It gains each
   stream's newest moment beside its lines, in memory, with no stored change: §3.6's one new memory.
2. **After the window restarted** with the part still working, nothing was heard before the start. The view says *nothing heard
   since the window opened at 14:02*, never a moment it did not hear.
3. **The watcher's hearing** of a driven session (`SessionWatch.cs:183-189`), where the window holds no stream for the part.
4. ~~Its lineage's lines, newest `at`.~~ *(Withdrawn in round 4, O3-F5.)* Every connector call writes two lineage lines (§1.1), so
   the newest `at` would move the held answer at the call rate, the cost §4 rejects for *last heard*; the three sources above are
   what the window heard, and where none holds the view says *nothing heard since the window opened*. Round 2 also read the part's
   events here; round 3 left them out (C2.4).

*(R2-O5)* **How it reaches the page.** The three are facts of the window's memory, so they are pushed as a keyed partition of
the `calls` slice, `heard`, keyed by session and judged from memory as its `quiet` partition is (`CallsStateSlice.cs:37-41`), with
no `STATE_CHANGED`. A stream's new line wakes the slice, coalesced to one judgement per session per ten seconds, since the page draws
*1 min ago* by its own clock. The held answer carries no *last heard*. The head and the view read the slice's `heard` beside it.

The head's facts line for a working session gains one fragment where it has subagents or other parts working: *3 working with it ·
last heard 1 min ago* / *3 个在一起工作 · 1 分钟前有动静*, which opens the view. **The fragment gives way to *quiet*** wherever the
watcher has marked the session (`CallsStateSlice.cs:37-41`). The head never says two things about one session's hearing, and
D200's watcher is unchanged.

### 3.8 The data types

The read answers one type, fed whole to a presentational component (`Web/design/presentational.test.ts` covers `work/` and `asks/`).
*(C6.1, C2.5, C2.6)* Round 2's `coworkers` group, `channel`, `sitting` and `at` are gone from it: the first three are read beside it
(§3.9), and a composition moment would make every answer differ from the one held, so an unchanged answer would be told as moved.

**Row a's type** *(round 4: the scope this round settles)*. It carries nothing a call line alone moves (O3-F5), *(C5-2.2)* save
the *last seen* of a run rule 3 closed, which no line closed, so a call naming that run wakes the read (§3.6, §4.1); and nothing of
responsibility, waits, quests or co-workers:

```ts
type AtWorkHeld =                                 // what the page's query holds (O3-F8, C3-4.2, O4-F3)
  | { reading: true; at?: AtWorkAt }              // the pending answer, never `undefined`, which the query library fails;
                                                  // (O7-F2) with `at` where it was a numbered landing, the composition's own
                                                  // `{ pending, waits }`, compared by the number rule; without, where it was
                                                  // `HeldAnswers.Pending`, which carries no number, so any delivery replaces it (O5-F2)
  | { failed: { why: string }; at: AtWorkAt }     // a work failed with nothing held, or (C4-2.4) an input slice never judged
                                                  // and failing; never a query error (C4-2.3); the page asks nothing again
  | { answer: AtWorkA; stale?: { why: string }; at: AtWorkAt }; // stale: a refresh failed over this answer, or (C4-2.4) an
                                                  // input slice judged once is failing now (§4.1); (O6-F4) a query in
                                                  // error that still holds this answer is drawn as it, stale, with the error's words
type AtWorkAt = { epoch: string; n: number };     // (O5-F2) the number of the delivery that wrote it, kept in the query's own
                                                  // data, never beside the cache; written by the page, never composed
type AtWorkA = {
  of: { session: string };                        // row a asks for a session; an ask's view is the routing half's
  attended: string;                               // the part whose subagents are drawn: the one the page attends
  parts: AtWorkPart[];                            // the strip's parts, the attended one first, then the strip's order; at most 40;
                                                  // an attended record in no entry (an ended intake) is the one part (O4-F12c)
  moreParts?: Record<string, number>;             // parts past the bound, counted by the record's state
  subagents: AtWorkSubagents;                     // the attended part's
};
type AtWorkPart = {
  key: string;                                    // the record's id; opens its page
  agent?: string; mark?: string;                  // the maker's mark, never a hue alone
  state: string;                                  // (C4-1.2) the record's own `state`, worded `sessionState.*`; never the
                                                  // strip's shown word, which carries the reader's derived waits; no liveness
  children:                                       // its subagents, as for the attended part
    | { count: number; working: number; observed?: number; unplaced?: number; unreadable?: boolean }
    | { elsewhere: true };                        // (O5-F6) a teammate's part: *not visible on this machine*, never a count
};
type AtWorkSubagents = {
  read: 'read' | 'none' | 'unreadable' | 'elsewhere'; // §3.6: no file, read, or could not be read; (O5-F6) a teammate's
                                                  // part, whose lineage is on another machine, never read here
  unreadable?: string;                            // the error's kind
  harness?: 'claude-code' | 'codex';
  seenByCalls?: boolean;                          // a harness whose children appear only when they call Daoris (Codex)
  working: number;                                // never an `unsaid` run (O4-F1)
  turnWaits?: boolean;                            // `claude-code-acp` alone, a child working: its turn waits for them (O4-F1)
  observed?: number;                              // ids the hook, or a Codex part's calls, named that no announcement matched; never children
  unplaced?: number;                              // (C5-2.1) ids named by lines tied to no process (no `proc` after the file's
                                                  // first `start`, or a `proc` no `start` names); never children, rows or states
  children: AtWorkChild[];                        // at most 40, filled as §4.1's bound has it (C4-4.1): open runs, then lost,
                                                  // then the newest three of the rest, each only with its whole chain of ancestors
  folded?: number;                                // the rest past the newest three, none drawn as an ancestor: *n ended*
  more?: Record<string, number>;                  // every child not drawn for want of room, whatever its state, counted by state;
                                                  // rows, `folded` and `more` together count every child once (C4-4.1)
};
type AtWorkChild = {
  key: string;                                    // its native id
  type?: string;
  label?: { text: string; claim: true };          // the harness's announcement, the agent's words
  parentKey?: string; depth?: number;             // absent where no harness said: *parent not said*
  state: 'working' | 'unsaid' | 'completed' | 'failed' | 'cancelled' | 'disconnected' | 'stopped' | 'finished' | 'ended'
    | 'lost' | 'unrecorded' | 'uncertain';        // unsaid: open, its harness saying no end (Codex): never working (O4-F1);
                                                  // unrecorded: closed in a driven record's history, its end never written (C4-1.1);
                                                  // uncertain: open, its process told neither running nor gone: never working,
                                                  // never lost (C8-1.1)
                                                  // stopped: the stream's word (O5-F3); finished: an end with no word, or a word
                                                  // none of the wire's or stream's five (O5-F3)
  since: string; startSaid: boolean;              // its run's start, or its first line where no start was said: *first seen* (O3-F1)
  ended?: string;                                 // the end its harness or its process's `end` line said, with its moment
  lastSeen?: string;                              // only for a run rules 2 or 3 closed, an `unrecorded` one, or one begun in a
                                                  // generation already closed (O3-F11e, C4-1.1, C4-2.1); (C5-2.2) its last line
                                                  // before the line that closed it, never moved by a later one; (C8-1.1) and
                                                  // an `uncertain` one, its last line, moved by each later line of its run
  opens: { stream?: string };                     // its console tab while the window holds it
};
```

**The routing half's type**, as round 3 wrote it, which the later round extends row a's with:

```ts
type AtWork = {
  of: { session: string } | { ask: string };
  leadPart?: string;                     // the lead's record id, §3.1; never EntryAccepts.EntryLead (R2-O9)
  waitingOn: AtWorkEdge[];               // the lead's current wait, §3.2; one quest at most; none once answered (C2.1)
  groups: AtWorkGroup[];                 // in §3.1's order, an empty group absent; co-workers are read beside it (§3.9)
  hint?: 'not-driven' | 'no-lanes' | 'one-at-a-time'; // §6.4, judged in .NET by the routing standing (C2.5, R2-O11)
};                                       // no composition moment (C2.6)
type AtWorkGroup = {
  kind: 'parts' | 'subagents' | 'quests' | 'askedBy' | 'gathered';
  members: AtWorkMember[];               // at most 40 (C2.6): the lead, the live and working, the lost, then the newest
  folded?: number;                       // ended members past the newest three
  more?: Record<string, number>;         // members past the bound, counted by state (C2.6)
  observed?: number;                     // subagents: starts the hook saw that no announcement matched; never children (C4.1)
  unreadable?: string;                   // subagents: the lineage could not be read, and the error's kind (C4.1)
  seenByCalls?: boolean;                 // subagents: a harness whose children appear only when they call Daoris
};
type AtWorkMember = {
  key: string;                           // the record's own id: a session, a quest, a request, a child's native id
  kind: 'part' | 'child' | 'quest' | 'asker' | 'voice';
  agent?: string; mark?: string;         // the maker's mark, never a hue alone
  on?: { text: string; claim: boolean }; // claim: the agent's words (a child's label), drawn as such
  lanes?: string[];                      // a part's or a quest's lanes
  state: string;                         // the record's own state word; a child's end word as the wire said it
  waiting?: boolean;                     // its quest's current executor, its wait still standing: live, never folded (R2-O3, C2.1)
  answered?: boolean;                    // its quest's current executor, its question closed and its resume not begun (C2.1)
  taken?: 'conversation' | 'elsewhere' | 'unrecorded'; // a quest taken with no executor here (R2-O6, C1.2)
  since?: string; ended?: string;        // a child's: its newest run's (C2.3)
  heard?: { at: string };                // §3.7's fourth: the lineage's newest; the calls slice's `heard` wins (R2-O5)
  alongside?: boolean;                   // a part in a tree of its own, read from its tree (R2-O2)
  children?: { count: number; working: number; unreadable?: boolean }; // a part other than the lead
  routedBy: { door: string; by: 'session' | 'person' | 'ask' | 'chain' | 'harness' | 'workflow'; detail?: string };
  waitsOn?: AtWorkEdge[];
  depth?: number; parentKey?: string;    // a child's place in the tree; absent where no harness said
  opens: { session?: string; quest?: string; stream?: string; meeting?: string };
};
type AtWorkEdge = {
  to: string;
  kind: 'helper' | 'quest' | 'person' | 'untaken' | 'partner';
  label: string;                         // untaken: why it sits is the tick's, read beside (§3.9, C2.5)
};
```

Beside its answer each composition keeps, never sent, **its dependencies**: every input it read with the version it read (§4, C2.4,
O3-F2, C3-2.4).

### 3.9 What the view reads beside the read (routing half)

*(C6.1, C2.5.)* Each of these already has a reader, a wake and a presentation, and the view composes them with the read's answer
rather than reading the same records again:

| Block | Its reader, as it stands | What the view adds |
|---|---|---|
| *Co-workers* | D203's exchange read of the line, COWORKUX1a's (the coworker design §6.3), with its rows (§6.2) | nothing; its rows drawn read-only, opening the member's thread (COWORKUX1b) or, until it is built, the member's own session page (C5.3). Which helper a lead waits on is that read's open ask whose asker is the lead |
| why a quest sits | the tick's `considered`, held as `useConsidered` (`Web/bridge/driver.ts:337`) and told by `DRIVER_TICK` (`DriverLoop.cs:1027`), as the ask's work reads it (`AskWork.tsx:56`) | the sentence on an untaken quest's row, and on an answered part whose resume sits |
| the channel | the session page's `ChannelPanel` read (`AttendedSession.tsx:323-324`), the ask page's channel section | its newest three lines, placed on the member that posted each |
| *last heard*, *quiet* | the `calls` slice's `heard` and `quiet` partitions (§3.7) | nothing |
| a meeting or a brainstorm | its page (MEETUX1b, MEETUX1c), by link | its row's state, from the read (row f) |

Each verdict among them is judged in .NET once, as D182 point 6 has it: the tick's sentence is the planner's, the hint's standing
the routing judge's (§6.4). The page renders them.

## 4. How it updates without the window asking (NOBLOCK1)

### 4.1 Row a's read, whole (round 4; round 5's changes marked)

*(O3-F2, O3-F5, O3-F8, C3-1.4, C3-2.4, C3-4.2; C4-2.2 to C4-2.4, C4-3.1, C4-4.1.)* This is the whole of row a's update contract.
§4.2 is round 3's text, kept as the routing half's draft; where they differ, this holds for row a. §4.3 says where its code sits.

- **One read, `SESSION_AT_WORK`, `[ByEvent]`, keyed by a session.** `WindowCensusTests` gains it as told by event.
- **Its inputs are in memory or on this machine's disk; none is a service read** (C3-1.4). They are:
  - the entry's parts, from the `groups` slice's snapshot, its `entries` partition, which is the strip's own (§1.8); an attended
    record in no entry, an ended intake whose ask became quests, is its own one part (O4-F12c);
  - each part's record, from the `sessions` slice's snapshot: only the fields the answer reads, its state, adapter, `created`,
    *(C4-1.1)* `kind` and maker's mark, never its `note`, `ran` or `updated`, which move often (O4-F12d); *(O5-F9d)* an attended
    record the slice does not hold yet has no state to draw, so the read answers pending and registers the record as absent, as
    a file is registered absent, and the push of the partition that brings it wakes the read;
  - *(O8-F5; round 4's "which parts have a process running in this window, from the driver slice's `running`" withdrawn)* no
    `running`: rule 3 reads a generation's process by its token or its issuer, never by its session (§3.6), so the driver
    slice is no input of row a's read;
  - *(C7-2.1, O8-F4, O8-F5)* for each recorded generation (§3.6), where this Daoris process issued it, whether it still holds
    its token, from the token set in memory, and otherwise its issuer's answer, alive, gone or cannot be told, by Platform's
    lease test on its id and start time;
  - *(C4-2.4)* each of those two slices' health *(O8-F5: three until round 9)*, beside its snapshot (below);
  - each part's lineage file, read by name.

  So row a needs no burst owner for service reads: the slices judged them once for every window. Until the `groups` and `sessions`
  slices have judged, the read answers pending, never an empty answer (D182's PUSHUX1), unless the one not judged is failing
  (C4-2.4).
- **An input's health** *(C4-2.4)*. A slice that fails only logs and keeps what it judged (§1.8), so the read could not tell a
  failed input from a pending or a current one. Each slice gains its health, read from memory under its gate beside `Snapshot`
  (`StateSlices.cs:170`): whether its last judgement failed, and that failure's words. It tells `HealthMoved`, outside the gate
  and after its snapshot holds what the judgement left, whenever a judgement's failure begins or ends (its failing flag flipping,
  `:207-220`), even where the judgement pushed nothing. *(C5-1.2)* **And at its first judgement**, the move from not judged to
  judged, whether or not that judgement pushed a partition: a first judgement of no partition, as a sessions list with no record
  judges (`SessionsStateSlice.cs:102`), sets the slice judged (`StateSlices.cs:241`) and tells no `Pushed`, which follows moves
  alone (`:248`). Its health is so three facts, judged, failing and the failure's words, and `HealthMoved` is told when any of
  them moves, once each time: *(C6-2.6)* a second failure in a row with the same words tells nothing, and one whose words changed
  tells once, so the view says the new words. *(O5-F7)* A slice whose judge answers null, as both do until the service comes up
  (`SessionsStateSlice.cs:71`, `GroupsStateSlice.cs:111`), judges nothing and fails nothing, so it stays not judged and not
  failing, and the view stays *reading* there **by design** (D182's PUSHUX1: not answered is not none). The read takes each
  input slice's health with its snapshot, and no service call:
  - not judged, not failing: pending, as above;
  - not judged and failing: `{ failed: { why } }`, the slice's own words (*Who is at work could not be read: {{why}}*);
  - judged and failing: the answer composed from the snapshot the slice keeps, with `stale: { why }` (*Could not read again:
    {{why}}*, its rows kept, dimmed);
  - healthy again: the next composition carries no `stale` and lands as moved.

  Each slice's health is a dependency, so its `HealthMoved` wakes the read on a first failure, on a failure over a kept snapshot
  and on the recovery alike, with no ask of the page's own.
- **Its dependencies.** Each composition keeps, beside its answer and never sent, every input with the version it read:
  - each lineage file: its stamp, taken before the read (`HomeWrites.Stamp`), or *absent* where there is no file, since the first
    child's line, the root's first call or *(C4-2.1)* a process's `start` creates it; the length read **up to its last full
    line** (O4-F12b: the hook and the connector append from other processes, `lineage-hook.mjs:48-58`, `LineageFile.cs:104-120`,
    and the fold skips a half line, `ChildBindings.cs:335-346`, so the judge reads on from that line's start); the native ids its
    runs hold in each generation; the ids it counted as unmatched; and *(C4-2.2)* the ids the fold read as its part's root
    (Claude Code's session, Codex's threads named with no parent and the root its metadata names, `ChildBindings.cs:139-163`);
  - the entry as the `groups` slice's partition held it, or *absent* where the attended session is in no entry; each part's read
    fields as the `sessions` slice's partition held them; *(O8-F5; round 4's "whether each part was in `running`" withdrawn)*
    and *(C4-2.4)* each slice's health as it read it;
  - *(O4-F2)* **a pending composition registers too**: where the `groups` or `sessions` slice has not judged, the dependency is
    that slice's not-judged status, so its first judgement's `Pushed` wakes the read and the answer follows with no other ask. A
    view restored open as the window starts so leaves *reading* as soon as the slices judge, not at a focus re-fetch.
    *(C5-1.2; round 5's "its first judgement's `Pushed`" corrected)* It is that judgement's `HealthMoved` that wakes the read,
    pushed or not, since an empty first judgement tells no `Pushed`;
  - *(C5-2.1)* each lineage file's unplaced ids, and *(C5-2.2)* which of its runs rule 3 closed (§3.6), *(C8-1.1)* and which it
    read `uncertain`, beside its runs, unmatched ids and roots (above), so a call naming any of them is judged by what it would
    move;
  - *(C7-2.1, O8-F4, O8-F5)* for each recorded generation the composition read, where this Daoris process issued it, its
    token's presence in the token set as it read it, and otherwise that issuer (its id and start time) and the answer it read,
    alive, gone or cannot be told;
- **Checked as it lands: the source fence** (C3-2.4). A composition about to land compares every dependency with its current
