     processes'; *(C4-2.3, C4-3.1)* a work's failure reaching the page as a refusal, or a numbered delivery taken without its

     server file a record's later processes are handed again; *(C6-2.4; round 6's "a void process's `start`" replaced)* reading a `start` its record has not taken as a boundary, or *(C7-2.1; O7-F4's "a `start` taken only after its spawn" widened)* any later `start`, taken before its spawn or after it, as closing an untaken generation, or an untaken process read by its session's `running` or marker; *(O7-F1)* a `start` written after its spawn; *(O7-F7)* closing a recorded generation whose own `end` the file holds at anything but that `end`; *(O7-F2)* the composition's pending answer written without its number; *(C8-1.1)* reading an issuer's exit as its agent's, or a record's move as an untaken process's, or drawing *working* or *lost* where nothing proves a process runs or has gone; *(O8-F1)* ordering rule 2's closes by `start` lines rather than takings; *(O8-F3)* writing an `end` before its process's exit is read, or keeping a token set per registry; *(O8-F5)* reading a generation's process by `running`;
     *(C5-2.2)* moving *last seen* at a line after the one that closed its run; *(C6-2.5)* an event written into a query while its own first fetch is out; *(O5-F2, C5-2.3)* a number kept beside the query's
     data, or an event of another epoch installed.

**The routing half's rows, for a later round** *(round 4)*. Rows e, b, c, d and f stay proposed under their own standing (the
design's *Standing*): round 4 corrects in them what the reads found, marked below, and settles none of them. Their order holds:
e, then b after a, then c, d and f.
2. **COWORKVIEW1e — the routing told, from a standing the agent is told** (driver, canon, web-shell). *(R2-O12c: moved up, its
   letter kept so the notes' citations hold.)* *(C5.2)* Round 2 had its skill section land on its own, while the drive it reads was
   told to no chat; this row now builds that source first, so it depends on no view row for its first half. SELFASK1 has landed
   (C1.3), so it waits on nothing else. Its half after row a: §6.4's three hints, which read the lead's lineage.
   - **The routing standing** (§6.4): one judge in .NET from the drive, the trees and the registry's lanes, and the driver's line
     told to each lead and chat at each start of its process, in BRWHINT1's places (§6.2, C3.1). No line for a helper, partner,
     reviewer, Ask Daoris, an intake or a brainstorm's pass (C3.2).
   - **The skill section**, through canon and then this repository and both examples synced, under the canon-authoring rules: who
     it is for (C3.2), the way read from the line told and unknown without one (C3.1), and R2-O2's answer (a lead waits on a lane
     part for its answer, never its code, and commits before it waits).
   - *Proof:* the judge's table (*open*, *one at a time*, *no lanes*, *not driven*, in R2-O11's order); the line in a new chat's
     first message, in a driven session's instruction, and at a go-on after the drive moved, with the moved standing; none for each
     role above; `daoris check` and the canon's tests. The hints' stories, and their rule's tests: each hint drawn at two subagents
     in one generation, as the first of the three that holds; `no-lanes` drawn for a driven repository with no lanes and no trees of
     its own; none drawn with lanes declared and driven with trees, or at one subagent; *(C2.5)* the hint moving when `driver.json`
     undrives the repository, and when the registry gains its lanes, with no session or quest written.
   - *(Round 4, for its round.)* The lanes-only proof passes only once `Repositories.Signature` carries the declared lane ids
     (O3-F3, C3-1.2, C3-5.2; §4.2). The one-at-a-time rule for a lead is tested apart from a chat's (C3-3.1). The start that tells
     the line keeps its coded note, and a standing moved since is drawn as *told: {standing}* (O3-F10).
3. **COWORKVIEW1b — a session's lane parts and the quests it asked, with their waits** (driver's read, web-shell; after a).
   - **What it builds.** The `parts` group's lane, *took* and *the ask's quest* routing (R2-O7), *alongside* read from the part's
     tree (R2-O2) and each part's subagents' count. The *Asked through quests* group from the quest units, with the takers (a chat's
     take, another machine's by `Holder`, and one by no session recorded here, R2-O6, C1.2), their current waits (C2.1) and, for a
     quest still `Open`, the tick's reason read beside the answer (§3.9, C2.5). The *Waiting on* line, one quest at most, gone once
     answered. *Asked by* (`Quest ?? TookQuest`; `Parent`; `From`). The `published` line.
   - *Proof (the person's second):* **a session that handed rows to its lanes shows each part and what it waits on.**
     - *(R2-O12a; round 2's run corrected.)* Under a cap of 1 a driven publisher holds the one slot itself (`Planner.cs:904`,
       `:1301`), so no lane part could run alongside it, and the other repository's session needs that slot to have started. So the
       publisher is a chat, which holds no slot (CAPCOUNT1). An example repository with two lanes (family phase 4b's engine, `core`
       and `media`), driven with trees of its own, under a cap of 1. In this order: the chat publishes to another repository, whose
       session takes the quest and waits on a question; then the chat publishes to each lane. One lane quest is taken and runs
       alongside; the other is untaken while the cap is spent.
     - The view draws the taken lane's session **once, as a part**: the set of parts is the *Parts* strip's, one row each (R2-O7).
       The untaken one is under *Asked through quests* with *not taken yet: the concurrency cap (1) is spent*, and moves to *This
       session* when taken. The other repository's session shows *waits on #q of repo*. Each is drawn in en and zh.
     - *(R2-O3)* **A lead waiting on its lane part**, under a cap of 2: a driven lead publishes one lane quest, commits, waits on it
       and ends its turn. The view draws the lead as the lead, live and never folded, with *Waiting on #q (core lane)*, and the lane
       part once below it, *alongside* only where its tree is its own (R2-O2). *(C2.1)* Then the lane part finishes: the line goes,
       and once the resume starts, the resumed record is the lead and the record that waited is folded among the earlier parts.
     - The read's tests: a chain's step under its parent, never *by you*; an ask's quest under its ask; a chat's taken quest as
       its *Asked by*; a taken quest with no executor here read by its `Holder` (C1.2). The page's: *(C2.5)* a tick that changes an
       untaken quest's reason, with no session or quest written, changes the row's words.
   - *(Round 4, for its round.)* Row b now builds the parts' responsibility row a left out: the current executor, *live*,
     *waiting*, *answered*, the lead rule and the fold by liveness (§3.1). Its selector is `LastRun`'s own rule as one shared
     function, a chat's take after it, and a take this machine's (O3-F4, C3-1.1, C3-5.3); its tests add a resume its agent
     refused before any prompt and a stood-down replacement, each leaving the earlier responsibility in place, and a chat's take.
     The lane part *finishes* by closing its quest done, by an answer or its own *Accept* (ACCEPTALL1b); a done held for the
     person's yes keeps the lead waiting (O3-F11a). The cap-1 run's other repository is one not driven here, so its question does
     not take the one slot first (O3-F11b). Before the first tick, `useConsidered` is empty (`Web/bridge/driver.ts:333-340`), so an
     untaken row says *not taken yet* with no reason, never an empty `{{why}}` (O3-F11d).
4. **COWORKVIEW1c — who serves it, and what was said** (web-shell; after b and COWORKUX1a, whose prerequisite HELPERWAIT1c is
   built). *(C5.3, C6.1)* Round 2 promised a thread no row had supplied and offered to write the exchange reader here; both are
   D203's.
   - **What it builds.** The *Co-workers* block from COWORKUX1a's exchange read, its rows read-only (§3.9); each row opens the
     member's thread where COWORKUX1b has landed, and the member's own session page until then. The *Waiting on* helper's name from
     the same read. The channel's three lines from the panel's own read, each placed on the member that posted it. No box joins the
     `atWork` watch, and the read gains no co-worker or channel field.
   - *Proof:* a lead waiting on a helper shows *Waiting on Codex · helper* and the helper's row opening its session page, then, once
     COWORKUX1b is in, its thread; the view's co-worker rows and the head's section rendered from one query; a channel line placed
     on the part that posted it, with no second channel read; en and zh.
   - *(Round 4, O3-F7, C3-5.4.)* COWORKUX1a's exchange read holds the line's helper asks alone (the coworker design §6.3, §12 row
     1). Row c draws what that read holds: helpers first, reviewers once COWORKUX1c and partners once COWORKUX1e extend it, with
     no change here.
5. **COWORKVIEW1d — an ask's view, and its terminal twin** (driver host, web-shell; after c). §3.5 on the ask's page. *(C5.4)* Its
   twin, as D185's R5 asks (`D185.md:24-25`): `daoris-driver ask at-work <ask> [--json]`, routed before the ask's other words, as
   `ask channel` is (`src/Daoris.Desktop/Daoris.Desktop.Driver.Host/Program.cs:470-475` at `972b17577`, `:474-479` at

   - *Proof:* an ask with two entries and a channel: the section above *Quests*, its rows opening each entry; stories; the verb's

   - *(Round 4, O3-F11f, C3-5.5.)* The twin prints the read's answer, its groups and waits. The tick's reason, the co-workers and

6. **COWORKVIEW1f — brainstorms and meetings on their caller's view** (driver, web-shell; after BRAINSTORM1d and AGENTROOM1b; its
   links after MEETUX1b). §3.6 point 2. *(C5.5)* Its requests and states are dependencies of the composition, under the watch's
   brainstorms part (§4).
   - *Proof:* the request's fields written by each door; the *Gathered* group's rows, each linking to its page; a meeting's state
     alone moving (a round from frozen to running, no ledger write) moves the row.
   - *(Round 4, C3-5.6.)* A request made after the view is held changes none of the files it read, since meetings are found by
     listing their folder (`MeetingFiles.cs:76`). The read also depends on the listings of `brainstorms/` and

SELFASK1 is built (archived 2026-10-24), and this design changes nothing of it (C1.3). FABRIC-C1b's row is rewritten to point here
when this is accepted, since row a meets both its halves.

## 8. Rejected

- **A team or structure record**, or a lane on the session record: two stores for one fact (D173's guard, the coworker design §3.4,
  R2-O13a).
- **Who does what read from the conversation**: an agent's prose, a plan's text or a tool card's words, parsed. A record's field or
  nothing.
- **A model's summary of who is at work** (D24).
- **`updated` advanced on every event**: it would break *ran* and *waiting* and write the store at the agent's rate (§3.7).
- **Native children as co-workers**: they have no record, no confinement, and nothing Daoris can stop by itself (D203 point 1 stands).
- **The child's prompt kept whole**, or the hook writing labels: a bounded label from the harness's announcement only, and the hook
  ids only (§3.6).
- **An end written at the session's end for an open child** (round 2): it would fold as the harness's own stop and read a killed
  root's child as *finished* (§3.6).
- **A lane part drawn twice**, as a part and as a quest's member (round 2): one record, one home (§2 point 4).
- **A group named for a turn, and a stored turn line** (round 2; its reason corrected by R2-O4). The read offered a driver line at
  each turn's end as the alternative. The event log already keeps each turn's end (§1.7), so a stored line would be a second store
  of it, and a group by turn would join the events and the lineage by time, which §3.6 refuses. The subagents are the lead's
  agent's either way.
- **A `helpers`-shaped rule for self-published quests, now** (round 2): the person's drive and the cap already bound them as they
  bind every quest, trees deciding only alongside or after (§6.5, R2-O13e). It is left as a later row if wanted.
- **A watch part per attended line, added and dropped** (round 1's): `HomeWatch` offers no way to remove a topic (§1.8). That is
  its surface, not the watch's, since `ChangeWatch` has `Unwatch` (R2-O13c); one fixed part, its judge reading by name, is still the
  simpler choice and replaces it.
- **The `atWork` wake narrowed by session** (round 2's, R2-O1): no wake carries the session it is about, and an ask's read and a
  read drawing another part's state would be missed. Every held read is asked again (§4).
- ***Last heard* told through `STATE_CHANGED`** (round 2's, R2-O5): each telling forgets every read under way (§4). It is pushed
  by the `calls` slice.
- **A waiting end code read as a wait on its own** (round 2's live predicate, R2-O3 as written; C2.1): a resume leaves the record
  that waited `completed` with its code and the quest naming its question, so the predicate drew two live parts and a wait already
  answered (§3.1).
- **The hook's starts hidden where a door's ids never join** (round 2's, R2-O8's second half; C4.1): the view could then say
  *Nobody else is working on this* over a hook that saw starts. They are a count of starts (§3.6).
- **A lineage `end` written by the orphan sweep** (C2.2's other way): the record's own end, which the read already holds, covers
  every way a record ends without its process, and a second writer of the part's end would race a go-on's `start` (§3.6).
  *(O7-F4: that race no longer holds, since an `end` names its `proc` and is placed in its own generation wherever it lands.
  Still rejected, read at `74725d604`: the sweep, `Trees/Orphans.cs:63-90`, is Workplace's (`fixtures/structure.json:703`),
  below Participants, so it would write through a port of its own; it holds no process's token, which lived in the process that
  went, so it would find the generation to end by reading the file, a second reader of the lineage beside the fold; and the
  window it would close, a generation with no `end` reopened at its record's next move, is narrowed by the `start` written at the
  move and, for an untaken one, closed by it (§3.6, §9).)* *(C7-2.1: an untaken one is closed instead by rule 3's reading of its
  own process, once a later taking says whose run the record's `working` is, at the same moment.)*
- **One `Closed` flag reopened by any line** (today's fold; C2.3): a stop won for good, and a late call reopened a finished child.
  A run begins only at a start the harness said (§3.6).
- **The routing standing read by the service or the connector from the driver's configuration** (C3.1): this machine's drive is
  the desktop's, and the service answers no machine's facts; the driver tells it (§6.2).
- **The tick's reason and the driver's configuration composed into the held answer** (round 2's `sitting`; C2.5): each tick would
  then have to wake every held read, though the page already holds the tick's sentence (§3.9). The hint's standing is the one
  configuration fact the answer keeps, told by the driver slice's push (§4).
- **The co-workers, the boxes and the channel read again by this read** (round 2's; C6.1): a second reader of what D203's exchange
  read and the channel's panel already hold, with its own wakes.
- **The smaller shape as links alone** (C6.1, the part not adopted): the person's case was work spread over places nobody looked at
  together, so the parts, the subagents, the quests and their waits stay drawn in one place, each by its existing presentation,
  rather than reached only from their own pages.
- **A composition moment in the answer** (round 2's `at`; C2.6): every composition would differ from the one held, so each would
  be told as moved.
- **Settling both halves in one round** (the steward's call, round 4). Two rounds of Codex's reads each found more than ten
  blocking points, most in the routing half's responsibility, wakes and doctrine, and each round's answers opened new ones there.
  A design that wide is never accepted, and the owner's case, a chat's subagents seen, waits behind it. So row a is specified
  whole and settled alone, and the routing half keeps its own standing and reads. Rejected with it: dropping the routing half,
  since the owner's outcome includes routing better, and settling it by a vote of the readers.
- **A run opened only by a start the harness said** (round 3's; O3-F1, C3-2.1): Codex says no start, so every Codex child was
  erased (§3.6).
- **A generation closed by the next `start` alone where processes write no `end`** (round 3's; C3-2.2): a clean process's open
  child would read *lost*. Every process is bracketed (§3.6).
- **A last generation closed by whether the part is live** (round 3's; C3-2.3): a crash after a waiting record's conclusion left
  its child working. The process decides (§3.6).
- **A dependency compared with its composition's stamp at every judgement** (round 3's; O3-F2): it woke again until recomposed,
  and for good where no page asked again. Each read wakes once (§4.1).
- **Records left out of the dependencies** (round 3's; C3-2.4): a ledger-only stale landing went unseen. The fence checks every
  input (§4.1).
- **Row a reading the service per burst through `RouteFlights`** (round 3's; C3-1.4): flights that do not overlap share nothing,
  and the slices already hold the records (§4.1).
- **The lineage's newest moment as *last heard*, and call lines waking the read** (round 3's; O3-F5): the connector writes two
  lines a call, the rate R2-O5 kept *last heard* from (§3.7, §4.1).
- **`undefined` as the pending answer** (round 3's; O3-F8): the query library fails it.
- **A refresh's failure kept silently over a held answer** (round 3's; C3-4.2): the view would show a stale answer as current
  (§4.1, §5.2).
- **Unmatched hook evidence counted as starts, or beside an empty sentence** (round 3's; O3-F9, C3-4.1): an id seen only by its
  calls went uncounted, and starts nobody ended were drawn beside *nobody*. Ids are counted, and the count stands in its place.
- **An open run whose harness says no end drawn *working*** (round 4's; O4-F1): a Codex child would read working for the whole of
  a chat's process. It is `unsaid` (§3.6).
- **A file's lines all one generation until a `start`** (round 4's; O4-F4): every file written before row a, the owner's case
  among them, has no `start`. An `end` bounds a generation (§3.6).
- **A failure that makes the page ask again** (NOBLOCK1's path today, which round 4 took as written; O4-F3): a work that always
  fails was begun again at every failure. *Try again* is the only re-ask for this read (§4.1).
- **`running` read as the machine's** (round 4's; O4-F6): it is this window's; another process's part closes by its record.
  *(O8-F5: and from round 9 no generation is read by `running` at all, below.)*
- **Every file written before row a read as one `end` per process** (round 4's; C4-1.1): a driven record's continuation and
  retry wrote none, so that `end`'s word and moment were another process's. A driven record's history is read as unrecorded.
- **A late capture line dropped at the writer** (the other fence C4-2.1 offers): it would hide a child its harness announced,
  which D173's ADR-B forbids. The line keeps its process's token and is placed in its own generation.
- **A line with no token placed by its moment** against the `start` it follows: the fold joins nothing by time (§3.6), and a
  clock read twice is no order. Rule 1's measure counts such lines instead, so the residue is a number, not a guess. *(C5-2.1)*
  Nor by its position after a `start`: it is unplaced (below).
- **Telling `ANSWERED` under `HeldAnswers`' gate** (C4-3.1's other way): every landing would hold the gate across the bus's emit,
  the stall NOBLOCK1 removed. Numbers make any order of delivery safe, as D182 point 3 has the slices.
- **Every held read made a value and numbered at once** (C4-2.3, C4-3.1): today's reads are tested on a refusal and a failure's
  re-ask; this read is the first numbered one, and another is numbered by a row of its own.
- **Every working child kept past the bound** (round 4's, from O3-F11c; C4-4.1): forty-one cannot fit forty, and a chain deeper
  than the bound cannot fit at all. The bound fills by chain and counts what it leaves.
- **A line with no usable token placed by its position after a `start`** (round 5's; C5-2.1, C5-3.1): an old stop sitting after
  P2's `start` could end P2's reused X, and an old start make a working child there, a definite state from evidence of no definite
  process. Such a line is unplaced: counted, never given a state (§3.6).
- **The token in the pipe door's server file** (O5-F4's other place): a driven record's file is handed again to its continuation and
  its retry, so it would name the first run's process for every later one. The pipe door's harness inherits the spawn's
  environment instead.
- **The `start` written only once the record is the run's** (the other way past O5-F1): the lines a process wrote before its
  advance, the live first resume's earliest among them, would name a token no `start` named yet and so read unplaced until it
  came, and a refused advance would leave its process's lines unplaced for good. The duplicate's `end` says `void` instead.
  *(Round 7: still rejected. The `start` stays at the spawn, and the taking is said beside it, `took` on the `start` or a line of
  its own, so a process's lines are placed from its `start` whether or not its record takes it. O7-F1: before the spawn at every
  place, a record's start's included; O7-F4: at the move, for a run moved first.)*
- **A call naming a closed run waking its read** (C5-2.2's other way, for every close): *last seen* frozen at the line that closed
  the run makes the call move nothing, so no wake is owed. Only a run rule 3 closed, which no line closed, is woken by its call.
- **The page's number kept beside the query's data** (round 5's; O5-F2): it outlived a query the cache collected, and dropped the
  remount's answer of the same number, leaving a false *failed*.
- **An event of another epoch installed** (round 5's "another epoch replaces"; C5-2.3): a late event of an earlier life of the held
  answers would restore its answer over a newer one. The page asks again, as a slice does.
- **A void `end` as the only mark of a process its record never took** (round 6's, from O5-F1; C6-2.4, C6-3.1): from that
  process's `start` until its void `end` landed, a composition drew the live run's children *lost*, and a void `end` the file
  refused left them so for good. Writing it sooner, at the refusal (O6-F1's way), narrows that window and keeps it. The taking is
  marked instead, so no `start` closes anything its record has not taken (§3.6).
- **Every `start` written once the launcher has answered** (O6-F1 as written, for the four spawns alike): where the record moved
  to the run before the spawn, a generation with no `end` before it would read open from the move until after the start gate,
  where round 6's `start` closed it before the spawn. There the `start` stays before the spawn, taken; after the launcher only
  where the record may still refuse the process (§3.6, §9). *(O7-F1: rejected for a record's start too. Under the taking an
  untaken `start` closes nothing, so a failed spawn's `start` costs only an empty untaken generation, which rule 3 closes once
  the record fails; written after the launcher, a line the token missed could land before a new file's first `start`, read as
  history and closed `unrecorded` when the `took` landed, a working child read as *its end was not recorded*.)*
- **The record naming the process it was moved for** (the other way to close §9's window at a record's next run): a field on the
  session record, written with each move to `working`, which the read would compare with the lineage. It is a second record of
  what the `took` says, kept by the service for a fact only this machine's lineage reads, and the window it closes needs a crash
  or a refused write first. The window is named instead (§9). *(O7-F4: and narrowed to the move's return, the `start` of a run
  moved first written as its move returns.)*
- **Any later taken `start` closing an earlier untaken generation** (O7-F4's other reach): a record's start's `took` can land
  while a refused process whose `start` precedes it in the file still runs, before its refusal's `end`, so its children would
  read *lost* while they run, the false state C6-2.4 withdrew. Only a `start` taken before its spawn closes one, since it follows
  a move the ledger makes only once the record's run has let it go, or the exit of the record's last process (§3.6, §9).
  *(C7-2.1: nor does that one, below.)*
- **A `start` taken before its spawn closing an earlier untaken generation** (round 7's named changes, O7-F4's first form; C7-2.1,
  C7-3.1): such a `start` follows the run that held the record letting it go, which says nothing of a competitor held before its
  advance, awaiting the service after its spawn and at its advance (`Driver.cs:3673`, `:3681`, `:3831`). The run that took the
  record can finish and its continuation spawn (`DriverContinuations.cs:1242`), or its record conclude and its next run be moved,
  while that competitor's children run, and they would read *lost*, against D173's truthful partial visibility. An untaken
  generation closes at its own `end`, or by rule 3 read for its own process (§3.6).
- **An untaken process read by its session's `running` or marker** (rule 3 as a taken generation reads it; C7-2.1): both are
  keyed by the session, a second start's `Track` replacing the first's entry and either's `Untrack` removing it
  (`SessionProcesses.cs:408`, `:668`), its marker written over and deleted alike (`:623-625`, `:640`). Once the run that took the
  record has ended, a competitor held before its advance is in neither, and its children would read *lost* while they run. Its
  own token is read instead, held by the Daoris process that issued it (§3.6).
- **`SessionProcesses` keyed by process for every reader** (C7-2.1's other way): stops, conversations, the watcher and the orphan
  sweep read it by session, and each would change for a fact only the fold needs. The tokens sit beside it, each its own entry.
- **A marker per process on this machine's disk** (the other way to read an untaken process another Daoris process runs): a second
  family of files beside the sessions' markers, written only once the spawn has given the process an id, so a process between its
  `start` and its marker would read gone. The `start` names its issuer before the spawn instead, and that process gone is its
  agents gone (§3.6). *(C8-1.1: the last clause withdrawn. An issuer gone says nothing of its agent, and a generation it issued
  reads not known, §3.6; the marker per process stays rejected, since it would prove an agent's exit only by a further write after
  the spawn, the new evidence round 9 declines, below.)*
- **A state of its own for an untaken generation's children past the run that took its record** (C7-2.1's other answer, an
  uncertain state): while its process lives its children are working, which *working* says truly; where its process cannot be
  told gone, they read as their harness says, in the states the design already names, *working* or `unsaid`, and §9 names where
  that outlasts the process. *(C8-1.1, C8-2.2, C8-3.1: withdrawn in round 9 by the steward's direction. Its reason was the false
  state Codex's read of round 8 found: an issuer gone with its agent running read *lost*, and an issuer that cannot be told read
  as running. The state is adopted, `uncertain`, *not known whether it still runs* (§3.6, §5.4).)*
- **A mark of containment on the `start`** (round 9's other way to read an issuer gone as its agent gone; C8-1.1, C8-2.2): with it,
  a contained process whose issuer went could read *lost*. Whether a start was contained is decided inside the start
  (`Platform/AgentStarts.cs:33`), after the `start` is written, and ORPHAN1's fallback job is joined only at the `Track` after
  the spawn (`Sessions/SessionProcesses.cs:405-406`), so the mark would be a further line after the spawn, written by a process
  that may go before it: new evidence, where the steward's direction for round 9 prefers the not-known state. Without it a
  contained process whose issuer went reads not known, as an uncontained one does, unless its record proves it (§3.6).
- **An untaken process read by its record's move** (round 8's record half; C8-1.1, O8-F2): its record was never its process's,
  so the record's move past `working` proves nothing of it, and where its own token is read the record adds nothing.
- **`queued` and `starting` read as not running an untaken process once its issuer is gone** (O8-F2b's first way): it would
  close the generation `lost` at its issuer's exit, which proves nothing of its agent (C8-1.1). The generation reads not known,
  and §9 names the record left `starting`.
- **Rule 2 by `start` lines** (rounds 3 to 8; O8-F1, C8-2.3): a generation taken by a `took` written after a later run's taken
  `start` was closed by that `start` while it ran. Rule 2 reads takings in the file's order, each where it sits.
- **A token set per registry** (round 8's placement; O8-F3): a registry is made per loop and per loop context handed none, so a
  set per registry would read another registry's live tokens of the same process as gone. The set is the Daoris process's one.
- **A generation's process read by `running`** (rounds 4 to 8, for a taken one; O8-F5): `running` is keyed by the session, so a
  second start's `Untrack` removes the live run from it, and a token or an issuer is the process's own. With it the driver
  slice leaves row a's read.
- **A chat's `end` written before its conclusion on every path** (O7-F7's other way): it moves one line on each of a chat's
  conclusions (`ChatRunner.cs:2935`, `:2954`, `:2963`, `:2967`, and a refused go-on put back at `:2916` and `:2948`) and leaves a driven
  run's `end`, which follows its conclusion too (`Driver.cs:3894-3906`, then `:3910`). The fold, which reads the whole file
  first, closes a recorded generation at its own `end` wherever it sits, which covers both (§3.6).
- **An event written into a query whose own first fetch is out** (round 6's "a delivery to a query whose data carries no number
  is always taken", for an event; C6-2.5): with no data held, an invalidation joins the fetch in flight rather than cancelling it,
  so its reply, of an earlier life of the held answers, replaced the event. The event waits for that reply, as a slice's push
  waits for its baseline (§4.1).
- ***Team at work* and `sessions team` widened** (round 1's): D203's word and verb (§5.4, §7).
- **A second running view or a free graph of who is at work**: the running view is GRAPHVIEW1's, in *Workflow*. This view is a list,
  which reads at every width (the flowchart design §8).
- **Daoris splitting a chat's roadmap into quests by itself**, or a coordinator agent dispatching the parts: D193's rejection, D203
  §3.4.
- **A poll** of the lineage or the boxes from the page: NOBLOCK1's rule.
- **The hint shown everywhere a way is not open**: most chats need none. It is said where subagents ran a roadmap (§6.4).

## 9. Not built, and unmeasured

- **Unmeasured:**
  - whether `SubagentStart` reaches the hook over the adapter, and how many `acp` ids join a hook id on a first run and a re-run
    (row a's first step);
  - the view's budgets in zh at 300 px;
  - how many lineage lines a long process writes, and so the fold's cost per wake.
  - *(R2-O13b)* Round 2's last item, whether a chat counts as its repository's active session for `one-at-a-time`, is answered by
    the code: it does (§6.4).
  - *(C2.6)* whether 40 members a group is the right bound, and how long a 40-member answer takes to serialise on the install;
  - *(C3.1)* whether agents told their routing standing hand rows out as the skill says, which only the install's sessions show.
  - *(Round 4)* how often a held read wakes on the install once call lines wake only for a new run and each read wakes once;
    row a's first week of the install's machine log says it, beside the measure in D173's note.
  - *(C4-2.1)* whether `DAORIS_LINEAGE_PROC` reaches the hook and the connector on each door, and so how many of their lines the
    fold places by position, and how many of those sit after a `start` their own moment precedes (rule 1). *(C5-2.1)* Since round
    6, how many it leaves unplaced, which costs a count and never a state; on Codex's protocol door, whose connector is handed the
    token in its entry, the expected count is none.
  - *(O5-F1; round 7's, C6-2.4, O6-F1)* a lineage write the file refuses is lost unsaid (`ChildLineage.cs:108-111`), and nothing
    counts refused writes today. A lost `start` leaves its process's lines unplaced, and a lost `end` leaves its generation to
    rules 2 and 3, Daoris's reading. A lost `took` leaves its `start` untaken: its own generation is still read by its lines and
    closed by its `end` or rule 3, but it closes no generation before it, so one with no `end` there stays to rule 3 while the
    record works. *(C7-2.1; round 7's named changes' "where its own `end` is lost too, the next `start` taken before its spawn
    closes it `lost`" withdrawn; C8-1.1: round 8's "rule 3 closes it once its process is gone and its record is past it"
    replaced)* Where its own `end` is lost too, rule 3 closes it `lost` once its token, in the window that issued it, has seen its
    process's exit; where that window went first, it reads not known for good. *(O8-F2a; round 8's "once its process has ended,
    it is closed where the run that took the record took it after the refused run's `start`; where that run took it before, the
    refused run's open children … read open until the record leaves `working`" replaced)* A refused run's lost `end` leaves its
    untaken generation to its token the same way, whichever run took the record first: closed `lost` once its process's exit
    has been seen. Read from another Daoris process while its issuer lives, it reads as its harness says until that issuer
    exits, and not known from then (below). Round 6's lost void `end`, which read a live run's children *lost*, is gone with the
    void `end`: no refused write now closes a live generation, and the race leaves no window while every write lands (§3.6).
    *(O8-F1, C8-2.3; round 8's "Nor does any order of the processes: an untaken generation reads open while its process runs,
    whatever its record and the other processes do" corrected: under rule 2 by `start` lines a competitor admitted late as a
    go-on was closed by the continuation's earlier taken `start` while it ran)* Under rule 2 by takings no order of the
    processes closes a generation while its process runs: an untaken one closes only at its own `end` or its token's proof, and
    a taken one at the first taking after its own, which follows the exit of the process it took, but where the session's
    registry lets a live run's record be stopped (below).
  - *(Round 7, found in its check of the code and raised by neither read.)* **A generation with no `end`, reopened at its record's
    next run.** A generation no `end` closed (the application's crash with a child open, or a refused `end`) is closed by rule 3
    once its record leaves `working`, the orphan sweep's `stopped` among them. Where the record is then moved to `working` for its
    next run, a resume or a chat's go-on, rule 3 no longer holds, and until that run's taken `start` lands the generation reads
    open, its open runs drawn *working*: from the move (`DriverContinuations.cs:272`; a chat's ledger move before its spawn,
    `ChatRunner.cs:2892`) to the `start` written before the spawn (§3.6). Round 6 had the same window to the same line, and round 7
    keeps it no longer, writing a taken `start` before the spawn rather than after the launcher. Closing it needs the move and the
    word of which process it was for to be one write, a stored field §8 rejects for row a. *(O7-F4, read at `74725d604`; round
    7's "to the `start` written before the spawn" narrowed, and its citation of a chat's move corrected, O7-F8.)* **Narrowed to
    the move's return**: a run moved first writes its taken `start` as soon as its move returns (`DriverContinuations.cs:278-285`;
    a chat's go-on `Conversations/Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:667-674`, Ask Daoris's `:756-761`), not after the preparation and the start
    gate (a resume's tree lock among them), which took long enough for the `sessions` slice's push to land inside the window. What stays is the
    moment between the service's move and the driver's next line, which a composition meets only where that push lands in it.
    *(C7-2.2)* That moment holds the service's answer to the move on its way back, an awaited HTTP response
    (`Driver/Service/ServiceClient.cs:1857`), so rule 5 proves the reading from that `start` on, not across the moment, which is
    named here and not closed.
    *(C7-2.1; round 7's named changes' "is closed by that `start` too" withdrawn)* An untaken generation with no `end` (a crash
    between the advance and its `took`, or a lost `took` and a lost `end`) is read by its own process instead (§3.6).
    *(C8-1.1, O8-F5, read at `103fc1aa6`; round 8's "it is closed once its record is past it … So it reads open at a later run
    only for the same moment" replaced)* **The window draws not known, never *working*.** A generation the reading Daoris process
    issued is read by its token, which has seen its process's exit, so it never reopens at a later run. One whose issuer went,
    the crash's, is read by its record only where it is taken: closed while the record is past `working`, and from the record's
    next move to `working` until that run's taking lands, its open runs read not known, the issuer gone and the record's
    `working` perhaps another process's. An untaken one whose issuer went reads not known throughout, never *working* at a later
    run. So the moment §9 names holds a true state, though it is not closed.
  - *(C7-2.1; round 7's named changes' "a refused process outlasting the run that took its record" gone with the closure it
    named)* **Where an untaken process cannot be told gone.** No later `start` closes an untaken generation now, so a refused
    process held past the run that took its record reads as its harness says until its own `end` (§3.6), and nothing of that
    window remains. What remains is where a process's end cannot be read. An untaken process another Daoris process on this
    machine started is read by whether that Daoris process lives, since this window cannot read its tokens: where its `end` is
    refused, its children, ended with it, read open until that Daoris process exits. *(C8-1.1, C8-2.2; round 8's "And an agent
    the system made no job for (`AgentJobs.cs:46-49`) may outlive the Daoris process that started it, which rule 3 reads gone once
    that Daoris process has gone and its record is past it" withdrawn: that drew a live child *lost*)* Once that Daoris process
    has gone, its generation reads not known, never *lost*, whether its agent went with it or not.
  - *(C8-1.1, C8-2.2, C8-3.1, O8-F2, O8-F4, read at `103fc1aa6`)* **Where a process can be told neither running nor gone, it
    reads not known.** Its open runs read *not known whether it still runs · last seen {time}*, which is true, in place of round
    8's states: a gone issuer read as its agent gone, *lost* while that agent ran, and an issuer that cannot be told read as
    running. What remains is how long the state lasts and where it is drawn:
    - **An untaken generation whose issuer went before its `end` reads not known for good**, through its record's later runs: a
      crash between a start's spawn and its `took` leaves one, and so does a crash before a record start's advance (O8-F2b) or a
      competitor whose Daoris process went before its refusal. It is drawn beside the lost, never in *working*, and a call of a
      surviving agent moves its *last seen*. Only a mark of containment could close it for a contained agent, which §8 rejects.
    - **A record whose Daoris process went before a record start's advance stays `starting` for good** (O8-F2b). The orphan
      sweep takes `starting` only on an opener's mark (`Trees/Orphans.cs:74-76`), and the spawn's `Track` has replaced that mark
      with the process's own (`Sessions/SessionProcesses.cs:409`, the opener's word read at `:589`). That is the sweep's
      (QUEUED1's), beyond row a; the view reads the generation not known, never *working*.
    - *(O8-F4)* **An issuer whose id a process with an unreadable start time has taken** reads not known, as any issuer that
      cannot be told, never as running, so no generation is held open on it.
  - *(O8-F5, read at `103fc1aa6`)* **The session's registry loses a live run when a second start of its record ends.** `Track`
    keeps one entry and one marker per session, so a competitor's `Untrack` removes the entry and deletes the marker while the run
    that took the record lives (`Sessions/SessionProcesses.cs:408`, `:668`, `:640`, `:675`). Another Daoris process's orphan
    sweep may then stop that live run's record (`Trees/Orphans.cs:74-81`), and this window's own stop finds no entry (`:223`).
    That is the registry's defect, beyond row a, for its owner to file. Row a no longer reads `running`: the run's own window
    reads it by its token and another window by its live issuer, both *working*, whatever the record says. Two places still rest
    on the record: once the run's issuer has gone too, the record's false `stopped` closes its generation by rule 3, and where a
    go-on then takes that record beside the live run, rule 2 closes it at that taking.
  - *(O7-F7)* **A generation's `end` written after its record's conclusion.** A chat's `end` follows its conclusion in its run's
    `finally`, and a driven run's follows its conclusion (§3.6), so a go-on's or a resume's taken `start` may land between them.
    Until that `end` lands, the earlier generation reads `lost` by rule 2 (*(O8-F1)* the go-on's or the resume's taking being the
    first after its own); once it lands, as its `end` says, wherever it sits.
    The gap is a few lines on the clean path, and up to the drain's bound on a chat's exception path.
- **Not designed here:**
  - DEV6's lane reservations and cap, and DEV9's steward;
  - a separate rule for self-published quests (§6.5);
  - *(R2-O2, the read's alternative)* a resumed lead's tree synced with the lane part it waited on, as `GoOnInSyncAsync` syncs a
    chain step with its source (`Driver.cs:549-575`). The doctrine's answer (§6.2) makes it unneeded; if it is wanted, it is a
    later row;
  - a child's own transcript after the window restarts (the console streams stay in memory);
  - *(C3.1; round 2 left it here)* a chat told whether its repository is driven here before it publishes is now designed: the
    routing standing's line (§6.2), built by row e. What stays undesigned is a standing told the moment it moves, mid-process: it is
    told at the next start (§6.2).

## 10. What it amends, once accepted

*(Round 4.)* Row a's acceptance amends D173 alone, and D118's frame by its side bar view. The D203, D115, D167, D176 and D177
amendments below come with the routing half's acceptance.

- **D173**: the FABRIC-C1a note's *ids only* is kept for the hook and the connector. The driver's lines gain a bounded label, a
  parent and the wire's end word, and the fold its moments and the generation join (§3.6). The file becomes transcript-class, kept
  on this machine (D47), since a label may name a path (R2-O13f). FABRIC-C1b's read and display are COWORKVIEW1a's, no longer after
  C2a. GRAPHVIEW1 stays the graph's half. *(Round 3)* The driver also marks each process's start, and the fold reads generations
  and runs, a last generation closed by the record's own end; a hook's unmatched starts are counted as starts; an unreadable file
  is never an empty fold (C2.2, C2.3, C4.1). *(Round 4)* Every process the driver starts for a part is bracketed by `start` and
  `end`; a last generation closes when the part's process is gone; a run begins at a said start or, for an id with none, at its
  first line; the hook's unmatched agent ids are counted (C3-2.1 to C3-2.3, O3-F1). *(Round 5)* Every line names its process by a
  token the driver binds into the process's writers, the `start` making the file, and a line is placed by that token; a driven
  record's history is read as unrecorded; a root's call wakes nothing; the read's failures are values and its deliveries
  numbered; its inputs' health is read; the bound fills by chain and counts what it leaves (C4-1.1, C4-2.1 to C4-2.4, C4-3.1,
  C4-4.1). The lineage's twin row gains `proc`. *(Round 6)* A line no process can be named for is unplaced, counted and given no
  state; the token reaches Codex's connector in its entry, composed per process, and never through a reused server file; a process
  its record never took ends void; *last seen* moves only while its run is open; the page compares a delivery with the number in
  the query's own data and never installs another epoch's event; a slice's first judgement wakes a pending read; `stopped` is an
  end word; a teammate's part reads not visible on this machine; *(O6-F6)* a chat's `end` follows its capture on every path; the
  terminal's verb bounds its wait; and point 1 of the round-4 note takes the record's own word (C5-1.1, C5-1.2, C5-2.1 to C5-2.3,
  C5-3.2, O5-F1 to F7). The lineage's twin row gains `void`. *(Round 7)* A `start` closes the generation before it only once its
  record has taken its process, the `start`'s `took` or a `took` line saying so, and round 6's void `end` is withdrawn; a `start`
  its record may still refuse is written once its process exists, and a refused run's `end` at the refusal; the fold's first pass
  reads every line for identity alone; the page holds an event while the query's own first fetch is out, and draws a query in
  error over a held answer stale; the verb says which wait it gave up on (C6-1.1, C6-2.4 to C6-2.6, C6-3.1, O6-F1 to F4). The
  lineage's twin row gains `took` in place of `void`. *(Round 7's named changes)* Every `start` is written before its spawn, a
  record's start's untaken until its `took`, and a run moved first writes its taken `start` as its move returns; a `start` taken
  before its spawn also closes an earlier untaken generation no `end` closed; a generation's own `end` closes it wherever it
  sits; a brainstorm's pass writes a taken `start` and its `end`, which nothing reads; the composition's own pending answer keeps
  its number on the page; the verb asks again itself, reading which wait from the last answer; `DAORIS_LINEAGE_PROC`'s name is
  declared once in the driver's lowest subdomain, the hook and the connector its twins (O7-F1 to F7). *(Round 8)* An untaken
  generation closes only at its own `end` or by rule 3 read for its own process, absent from this machine's processes by its
  token and its record not running it, never by a later `start`, so round 7's named changes' closure by a `start` taken before
  its spawn is withdrawn; the Daoris process that issued a token holds it until its process has exited and its `end` has been
  written or refused, every `start` naming that process; the read is woken as a token leaves and as another issuer exits; rule
  15's orderings run through the query's own installation (C7-2.1, C7-2.3, C7-3.1). The lineage's twin row gains `issuer`.
  *(Round 9)* A generation closes by Daoris's reading only where its process's exit is proven, read by its `issuer`: the
  reading process's token, which leaves only after the exit; for a taken one whose issuer has gone, its record's move past
  `working`; and where nothing proves it, its open runs read *not known whether it still runs*, never *lost* or *working*.
  Rule 2 closes by takings in the file's order; every `end` follows its process's exit; the token set is one per Daoris
  process; the issuer is read by the lease's test; `running` and the driver slice leave the read (C8-1.1, C8-2.2, C8-2.3,
  C8-3.1, O8-F1 to F5). The lineage's twin row gains nothing.
- **D203**: points 1 and 2 stand as written. The view draws the team (the entry's parts and its co-workers) and, beside it, records
  point 1 keeps out, each under its own heading. `sessions team` stays COWORKUX1a's. This design's verb is `sessions at-work`.
  *(Round 3)* Its co-workers are COWORKUX1a's exchange read and open COWORKUX1b's thread; the view never reads the boxes (C5.3,
  C6.1). The routing doctrine is for leads and chats alone (C3.2).
- **D115**: §2.2's *work for another session* is, under D167, a further part of the publisher's session where the lane is its own
  repository's. Its lanes are shown on that session's view, and a repository where the way is not open is told how (§6.4, with
  SELFASK1, built). *(Round 3)* An agent is told its repository's routing standing at each start, and hands rows to its lanes only
  where it was told the way is open (C3.1).
- **D167**: a lane quest to the session's own repository is a further part of the session that published it (point 2's origin
  rule), drawn once as a part, and landed by the entry's one *Accept*. *(Round 3)* A quest's current executor is its newest record
  here; a record its resume replaced is an earlier part, whatever its end code (C2.1). *(Round 4)* "Newest" is `LastRun`'s own rule,
  one shared function, then a chat's take, and only where the take is this machine's (O3-F4, C3-1.1).
- **D176, D177**: a request names who called it (row f).
- **D118's frame**: a side bar view, `atWork`, after *Workflow*.
- **These stand:** D24, D45, D46, D47, D56 (NOBLOCK1), D116, D137, D169, D186 and the flowchart design, D188, D193, D200.

## 11. Round 2: the read's findings and their answers

Every finding of the Opus foundation read of round 1 (`docs/2026-10-24-cowork-view-reads.md` §4), each checked against the code on main
at `426ed2b73`. This table is round 2's record and is kept as written; where round 2's read changed an answer (the `work` scope and its
narrowing, the turn, the strip's order, trees as a bound), §12 says so and the sections above carry the change. *Taken* means the design now says it. *Answered* means it is not taken as written, for the reason given. *Deferred*
means a later row carries it. The read's §1 to §3 corrections are findings below.

| # | Finding (blocking in bold) | Checked against | Answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **A lane quest to a session's own repository makes a further part of that session's line** | `SessionEntries.cs:33-37`, `:96-113`; `SessionLedger.cs:1599-1616`; `Planner.cs:1716-1759`; `D167.md:22-31`, `:73-82`, `:286-336` | **Taken**, the second way: such a session is a part, drawn once under *This session* with its lane, its routing and *alongside*, in the *Parts* strip's order. An untaken quest to the session's own lanes is under *Asked through quests* until taken (§2 point 4, §3.1). The subagents are read only for the lead (§3.1, with finding 7). The lead is the attended live part, else the newest live part by `created` then id. The entry rule's effect is stated in §1.2, §1.3, §1.9, §6.1 and §6.5, and the landing on the entry's one branch. The hint says *further parts of this session*. Row b's proof asserts one row per part and the strip's order. A D167 note is added beside D115's. |
| **2** | **The routing doctrine parks a driven lead on the person** | `Observation.cs:159-174`; `Quests.cs:96-102`; `QuestTools.cs:366-374`; `QuestExchange.cs:1454-1505` | **Taken** as named: a chat ends its turn; a driven session finishes its quest naming the quests it handed on, or waits on one and ends its turn, and never ends a turn holding its quest with nothing named (§6.2). §3.2 says a lead waits on at most one quest. The exchange refuses no wait on a quest to one's own lane, so the wait is open. |
| **3** | **The routing names no rule of the person's and no precondition** | `QuestTools.cs:148-163`; `QuestExchange.cs:108-113`; `HelperBox.cs:86-88`; `Planner.cs:1889-1891`, `:1964-1966`, `:2010-2021`, `:2048`; `D203.md:31-33` | **Taken**, the second way: the rule is the person's drive with trees of its own and the machine's cap, which hold today. The reason is that a quest to a driven repository starts under exactly these, whoever publishes it, and a helper's rule covers a turn outside the plan (§6.5). The skill section applies only where lanes are listed and the session is driven, or the chat's person asked (§6.2). The hint is one of three, naming a missing drive or one-at-a-time where the way is not open (§6.4). A `helpers`-shaped rule is rejected for now and left as a later row (§8, §9). |
| **4** | **None of §4's wakes reaches a held read** | `HeldAnswers.cs:20-22`, `:74-106`, `:170-177`; `held.ts:24-34`; `stateHub.ts:27-68`, `:93-100`; `StatePublisher.cs:258-264`; `HomeWatch.cs:196-213`, `:234-258`, `:270-280`; `QuestsStateSlice.cs:130-144`; `CallsStateSlice.cs:23-24` | **Taken**, the first way: every wake tells a new `work` scope on `STATE_CHANGED`, and the hub invalidates `SESSION_AT_WORK`, whose next ask runs the work and is told `ANSWERED` (§4). The ledger's families come through `MoveHeard` by the existing `HearLedger` seam. The lineage files come through one fixed part over `sessions/` hearing `*/lineage.jsonl`, its judge reading by name only the held sessions', in the window's own process as `ForProposals` is. The boxes are fixed parts of the same watch. The `calls` slice is no longer a wake: *last heard* comes from the streams (finding 8). |

that shows why, and no finding is disputed: each was confirmed in the code.

| 1.2 | No local executor does not prove another machine took the quest | `Planner.cs:33-37`, `:1746-1765` | **Taken**: a taken quest with no executor here reads *taken by {conversation}* through `TookQuest`, *taken on another machine* only where `Holder` names a machine other than `Here`, else *taken, by no session recorded here* (§1.3, §3.1, §3.8, §5.2-§5.4, row b). |
| 1.3 | SELFASK1 has landed | `QuestExchange.cs:539-552`, `:759-761`; `KnowledgeSearchTools.cs:326-330`; the archive's SELFASK1 entry | **Taken**: §0, §1.3, §1.9 and §1.10 say the old refusal in the past and the new one as built; §6.4 cites it; row e no longer waits on it; §7's last line and §10's D115 line say it is built. |

Rows a and b test wait, answer, replacement working, and answered before the resume; §8 rejects the end code read alone. |
| **2.2** | **An application crash leaves children falsely working** | `Orphans.cs:81`; `ChildBindings.cs:241-244`, `:278`; `ChildLineage.cs:118-123` | **Taken**: the driver writes a `start` line at each process's start on both doors, to an existing file only; a generation closes at its `end`, at the next `start` (`lost`), or, the last one, at the record's own end where it is not live (`lost` for `stopped` or `failed`, `ended` for `completed`), the record's state passed to the fold and nothing written (§1.1, §3.6). Row b tests a tick's reason moving with no write; row e a `driver.json` edit and a registry's lanes moving the hint. |

**Where Codex and the Opus reads disagree**, decided on the code at `972b17577`:
- **The Opus read's round-2 finding 3, a waiting part as live.** Codex is right that the predicate it named is not sufficient: a
  resume opens a new record and leaves the one that waited `completed` with its code (`SessionLedger.cs:1612-1616`), and the quest
  keeps naming its question after the resume (`Planner.cs:38-50`). The Opus finding stays right that a waiting lead must read as
  live; round 3 keeps that, read from the quest's current executor (§3.1).
- **The Opus read's round-2 finding 8, the hook's children hidden where a door never joins.** Codex is right on the suppression:
  hiding evidence lets the view say *nobody* over starts a hook saw, against D173's ADR-B (`D173.md:11-12`). The Opus finding stays
  right that they must never be counted as children twice. Round 3 keeps both: they are counted as starts, never as children, and
  never hidden (§3.6).
- **The verdict.** The Opus read would accept round 2 after named changes; Codex asks another round. Each of Codex's eleven blocking
  findings holds in the code it cites, several of them in the predicates round 2's named changes had just added, so the verdict is
  Codex's: another round, which this is.

**What stays owed.** Both readers' reads of round 3 (D191). The design stays proposed until both have read it and their blocking
findings are taken; until then nothing of it is built, and nothing carrying a row of it is staged. *(Both landed on 2026-10-25;
§14 answers them.)*

## 14. Round 4: the reads of round 3 answered

Every finding of the Opus foundation reader's read of round 3 and of Codex's (`docs/2026-10-24-cowork-view-reads.md`, *Opus
(foundation), round 3* and *Codex, round 3*), each checked against the code on main at `b4f096cc0`. Marked *(O3-Fn)* and *(C3-n.m)*
in the text. *Taken* means the design now says it, and where; *carried* means it is the routing half's, corrected in its text and
settled in its own round. No finding is disputed: each was confirmed in the code it cites.

**Where the two reads overlap, the stricter is taken** (the steward's direction):
- **Call-only children** (O3-F1, C3-2.1). The Opus read opens a run at the first call where the harness says no start; Codex's keeps
  any first-observed child with an unknown start and bars a late call from reopening a closed run. Codex's is the wider and is
  taken, for every harness the rows come from (§3.6).
- **The watch** (O3-F2, C3-2.4). The Opus read's wake-once and absent stamps, and Codex's fence over records as well as files: both
  are taken, as one judge over every input's registered version (§4.1).
- **The registry's lanes** (O3-F3, C3-1.2). Both of the Opus read's ways together, the signature carrying lane ids and the lanes a
  dependency compared at each tick, with Codex's proof of a lanes-only change; carried to the routing half, since row a reads no
  registry (§4.2, row e).

boundaries, 4.2's input owners, 5.1's further omissions) are its findings 1.1, 2.4 and 4.2 below. *Taken* means the design now says
it, and where; no finding is disputed, each having held in the code it cites.

| # | Finding (blocking in bold) | Checked against | Answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1.1** | **Historical files do not hold one `end` per process** | `DriverContinuations.cs:1242`, `:1299` start processes and write no line; `Driver.cs:3891`, then `:3907`, the record's one `end`; a chat's `end` in each run's `finally`, `ChatRunner.cs:2977`, after `:2896` | **Taken.** §1.1's claim corrected: one `end` per process holds for a chat's file alone. A generation a `start` began is *recorded*, one begun without is *history*; a history `end` is its process's only where the record's `kind` is a chat, and in a driven record's history, or a history generation the next `start` closes, a run no harness ended reads `unrecorded`, *its end was not recorded · last seen {time}*, never `ended`, `lost` or the `end`'s moment. `kind` joins the read fields. Rule 5's fixture is now the existing driven case, a first run and a continuation under one `end`, beside a chat's (§1.1, §3.6, §3.8, §5.2 to §5.4; rules 5, 7, 10). |
| 1.2 | The strip does not word the record's raw state | `PartsStrip.tsx:56`; `groups.ts:148`, `:159` | **Taken.** Row a keeps the record's own state, now said as `sessionState.*` (as `AskWork.tsx:111` draws a session) and as deliberately unlike the strip's derived words; rule 17 tests a part the strip shows `awaiting-helper` drawn *completed* (§3.1, §3.8, §5.2, §5.4; rule 17). |
| **2.1** | **Delayed capture lines have no process attribution** | `Driver.cs:3965-3985`, `:4004-4026` (an abandoned capture still observed); `ChildLineage.cs:145-152`, made per capture at `HarnessCapture.cs:123`; `AcpStreams` made per connection, `Acp.cs:1668` | **Taken, the smallest of the read's two ways that keeps evidence.** A token, `proc`, issued before each of the four spawns, on that process's `start` and `end` and on every line the driver writes for it, bound into its writers when they are made, so a capture that outlives its process writes only as that process; the same token on the spawn's environment as `DAORIS_LINEAGE_PROC`, set anew at each spawn and among `Adapters.Roles` (`Adapters.cs:1574`, `:1659-1665`), for the hook and the connector to copy. The fold places a line with `proc` in its own process's generation wherever it sits: a late start begins no generation, and an old stop never ends a reused id's later run. The `start` makes the file, so a first process has its `start` too. A line with no `proc` is placed by position, as all history is; whether the token reaches the hook and the connector on each door, and how many of their lines sit after a boundary they precede, is rule 1's measure. Dropping late lines at the writer is rejected, since it hides children (§3.6, §8, §9; rules 1, 4, 6, 9, 21). |

Rule 13 proves a hundred root calls cause no wake (§4.1; rule 13). |

Health is a dependency: never judged and failing reads *failed*, judged and failing *stale*, each with the slice's words, and the recovery lands as moved; the composer still calls no service. Tests of a first-read failure, a failure over a kept snapshot and the recovery, for both slices (§1.8, §4.1, §5.2; rules 10 to 12, 16). |

**Where the two reads meet, the stricter is taken** (the steward's direction):
- **A line's process** (C5-2.1, C5-3.1, O5-F4). Codex asks that no line be given a process where its token or its token's `start`
  is missing; the Opus read shows that Codex's connector cannot carry the token by its spawn's environment at all, so on Codex every
  child line would be placed by position. Both are taken: the token travels in the connector's entry where that entry is composed
  per process, and a line with no usable token or `start` is unplaced, counted, and given no state.
- ***Last seen*** (C5-2.2, O5-F9c). Codex offers freezing it or waking on it; the Opus read asks that it take only lines that change
  a run. It is frozen at the line that closed its run, and only a close no line makes is woken.

## 18. Round 7: both reads of round 6 answered

Both makers' reads of round 6, each at `7341acf9f` (`docs/2026-10-24-cowork-view-reads.md`, *Codex, round 6* and *Opus
(foundation), round 6*): Codex's, 9 findings, 3 of them blocking, *another round* for row a; the Opus foundation reader's, 8
findings, none blocking, *accept*, asking that its findings 1 and 2 be written into the contract before the row is filed. Each
finding is checked against the code on main at `abf03ea26`, the commit round 7 is written from. Marked *(C6-n.m)* and *(O6-Fn)* in
the text. *Taken* means the design now says it, and where; *answered* means no change is owed, with the reason. No finding is
disputed: each held in the code it cites, and where the code check found more, it is said below.

**Whether the cited lines moved.** Both reads cite `7341acf9f`. Between it and `abf03ea26` only FOUNDATION1's row mod-0 landed,
the structure gate: `git diff 7341acf9f abf03ea26 -- src/` touches test projects, their fixtures, the desktop's
`Directory.Packages.props` and its README, and no product code, and neither the design, the reads record, D173, D182 nor the
foundation design changed. So every line both reads cite holds at `abf03ea26`, their `Design:`, `Foundation:`, `D173.md:` and
`D182.md:` lines among them. Against round 6's own citations, made at `bdc1b03a2` (O6-F5): FOUNDATION1's rows u (`ba7f8e050`),
a-i (`c0ad819ba`), 1c (`4748ad294`) and mod-0 landed after it. Of the files this design cites, `Driver.cs` gains two lines at
`:1993` and one at `:2686`, so every line round 6 cites past `:2686` is three later: the spawn `:3658` is `:3661`, the race's
`:3824-3837` is `:3827-3840`, the `end` `:3907` is `:3910`, the servers file `:2720` is `:2723`, the connector `:4315` is `:4318`
and `:4523` is `:4526`. `SessionsStateSlice.cs` gains three at `:47`, so its null judge `:71` is `:74`, its `Failed` `:77` is `:80`
and its judgement's end `:102` is `:105`, its fields at `:41` unmoved. `SessionGroups.cs` and `Web/data/api.ts` changed only past the
lines cited. `ChatRunner.cs`, `DriverContinuations.cs`, `Acp.cs`, `AcpStreams.cs`, `ChildLineage.cs`, `ChildBindings.cs`,
`SessionProcesses.cs`, `Adapters.cs`, `KnowledgeConnector.cs`, `HeldAnswers.cs`, `StateSlices.cs`, `GroupsStateSlice.cs` and the
page's `held.ts`, `slices.ts` and `stateHub.ts` did not change. Round 6's text keeps `bdc1b03a2`'s lines, and §17 now says so; a
passage round 7 changes cites `abf03ea26`'s.

**What the check found beyond the reads.**
- **The race both reads analyse is a record's start's, not a resume's.** Rounds 5 and 6 took ANSWER2's comment at the abandon path
  (`Driver.cs:3836`) for the race's source: a duplicate resume spawning before its advance is refused. Since SAIDBACK1e (landed
  2026-10-16, before every read of row a) a resume moves its record to `working` before it spawns, a refused move returning before
  any spawn (`DriverContinuations.cs:249-299`), and holds its run with `movedFirst: true` (`:447`), which skips the advance after
  the spawn (`Driver.cs:3827`); the look also claims the record for one run (`Driver.cs:1071-1082`). The abandon path is reached by
  a run whose record moves after its spawn, a record's start, and leaves a process beside a live one only where two starts of one
  record overlap (§1.1). Round 7's contract does not rest on which runs can overlap: no `start` closes anything until its record has
  taken it.
- **A generation with no `end` reopens at its record's next run.** After a crash, or a refused `end`, rule 3 closes the generation
  once its record leaves `working`; a resume or a chat's go-on moves the record back to `working` before its spawn, and until that
  run's `start` the generation reads open. Round 6 had the window and no read raised it; §9 names it, and §8 says why round 7 does
  not close it.

**Where the two reads meet, the stricter is taken** (the steward's direction):
- **When a later process closes a generation** (C6-2.4, C6-3.1, O6-F1). The Opus read narrows the window: each `start` written
  once its process exists, the void `end` at the refusal, the rest named. Codex asks that there be none. Codex's is taken, by
  marking the taking rather than the refusal: a `start` closes the generation before it only once its record has taken its
  process, so no prefix of the file draws a live run's children *lost* and no refused write leaves them so. The Opus read's
  ordering is taken where its case lives, a `start` its record may still refuse, with that run's `end` at the refusal; where the
  record moved first, the `start` stays before the spawn, so the window §9 names is no longer than round 6's.
- **The page's first fetch** (C6-2.5, C6-1.1). The Opus read finds *one fetch's result at a time* holds, the library joining a first
  fetch rather than starting another; Codex finds the hole that join leaves, an event written while the joined fetch is out and
  then replaced by its reply. Codex's fence is taken, the slices' own shape.

## 20. Round 8: Codex's read of round 7 answered

Codex's read of round 7 with the Opus foundation reader's named changes (`docs/2026-10-24-cowork-view-reads.md`, *Codex, round
7*), read at `26ef34d80`: 8 findings, 2 of them blocking, which are one point, the closure §19 took from the Opus read's finding 4,
seen as a protocol (its 2.1) and as D173's rule (its 3.1), its §0 reopening round 6's 2.4 and 3.1 under it; *another round* for
row a. Every finding is checked against the code on main at `26ef34d80`, the commit Codex read and round 8 is written from, and
each place round 8 changes is marked *(C7-n.m)*, Codex's §n finding m. *Taken* means the design now says it, and where;
*answered* means no change is owed, with the reason. No finding is disputed: each held in the code it cites.

**Whether the cited lines moved.** Codex read the commit round 8 is written from, so none of its lines moved: `Driver.cs:3673`,
`:3827` and `:3831`, `DriverContinuations.cs:268` and `:1242`, `ServiceClient.cs:1857`, `HeldAnswers.cs:43`,
`StateSlices.cs:211`, `ChildBindings.cs:139`, `HarnessCapture.cs:123`, `held.ts:30`, `held.test.ts:55`, `stateHub.ts:75`,
`KnowledgeConnector.cs:114`, `StructureCheck.cs:205`, `D173.md:11` and `:5698`, and the foundation design's `:1225` read as it has
them, and its `Design:` lines are this design's before round 8's edits. Against §19's citations, made at `74725d604`:
FOUNDATION1's row mod-ii landed between them, Store's files moved into `Store/` and Platform's reads of Store carved out (D212's
note of 2026-10-25). No product file this design cites changed. The structure map's lines shifted where mod-ii moved Store's
entries and placed Platform's new files: `Trees/Orphans.cs` `:703` is `:705`, `Sessions/ChildLineage.cs` `:515` is `:510`, and the
census's `Permissions.cs → ChildLineage, LineageHook` `:1355` is `:1347`, `HarnessCapture → ChildLineage` `:1356` is `:1348` and
`Adapters → IntakeRoom` `:1395` is `:1387`; `:72`, `:75`, `:100`, `:126` and `:291` hold. §8's and §19's text keeps
`74725d604`'s lines; a passage round 8 changes cites `26ef34d80`'s.

**What the check found beyond the read.**
- **Rule 3, read as a taken generation reads it, fails Codex's ordering one step later.** `running` and the session's marker are
  keyed by the session: a second start's `Track` replaces the first's entry and either's `Untrack` removes it
  (`Sessions/SessionProcesses.cs:408`, `:668`), and the marker is written over and deleted alike (`:623-625`, `:640`). So once the
  run that took the record has concluded, a competitor still held before its advance is in neither, its record is past
  `working`, and rule 3 would read it gone while it runs. Withdrawing the closure alone would not answer Codex's point; round 8
  reads an untaken process by its own token.
- **A finished record can take a competitor's late advance.** The ledger takes a move to `working` of a record no longer active,
  asked with no expected state, as a go-on (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1723-1726`), which it refuses where no words wait
  (`:1821-1822`) and makes where they do. So a competitor held past its record's conclusion is refused or takes the record; rule
  5's case holds both. *(O8-F1, C8-2.3, read at `103fc1aa6`: it did not hold the second. Rule 2 as round 8 wrote it closed the
  competitor its late `took` took at the continuation's taken `start`, written before that `took`, drawing its live child
  *lost*; round 9's rule 2 reads takings in the file's order, and rule 5 states the case's prefixes, §21.)*
- **Every agent ends with the Daoris process that started it, but where the system made it no job.** The agents' job ends what
  it holds as that process's last handle closes (`Platform/AgentJobs.cs:39-43`), and a start not made through the launcher joins
  a job of its own as it is tracked (`SessionProcesses.cs:405-406`, ORPHAN1); a start that cannot be contained is made as it was
  (`AgentJobs.cs:46-49`). So an issuer gone is its agents gone, and rule 3's record half covers the start no job holds.
  *(C8-1.1 and the Opus read's §3 item 3, read at `103fc1aa6`: both halves wrong. A start the agents' job could not contain is
  still held by ORPHAN1's job, `SessionProcesses.cs:405-406`, which can fail too, `Platform/ProcessJob.cs:50`, `:53`, `:58`, so
  an issuer gone is not its agents gone; and a record an untaken process never held says nothing of it. Round 9 reads an issuer
  gone as nothing of its agent and draws such a generation not known, §21.)*

An untaken generation closes only at its own `end` or by rule 3 read for its own process: absent from this machine's processes, by its token (this window no longer holding it, or the Daoris process that issued it gone), and its record not running it (neither `queued` nor `starting`, nor `working` without a later taking in the file). The issuing process holds each token from its issue until its process has exited and its `end` has been written or refused, and every `start` names it, `issuer`.
