**The verdict.** Codex asks another round on one point, now taken: an untaken generation is closed by its own `end` or by
evidence that its own process is gone, read by its token and by the process that issued it, never by another process's lines or
its record's move, and rule 5 reads Codex's ordering at every prefix, held against fresh. Its smaller findings are a check each,
and 2.3's installation is in rule 15.

**The standing (D191).** Both makers' reads of round 7 have landed: the Opus foundation reader's named changes are taken (§19),
and Codex's one point is answered here. Whether row a needs another read, and whose: D191 has Codex read every design and the
Opus foundation reader read where a design changes a foundation, and a read may accept after named changes the steward can check
without another read. Round 8 is not such a change. Withdrawing the closure alone would be one the steward could check against
the code, but the check above found that it would not answer Codex's point, since rule 3 as a taken generation reads it draws the
held competitor *lost* once the run that took the record concludes. The answer is new mechanism: a field on the lineage's
`start`, a holding of tokens per process in the driver's process registry, a reading of another Daoris process's life, and two
wakes. That is a protocol between processes and a change to a record's data, a foundation by D191's amendment of 2026-10-20, and
each new protocol of row a so far has had holes that only a read found. So this round judges that row a needs both makers' reads
of round 8: Codex's, whose point it answers, and the Opus foundation reader's, since it changes the lineage file and a reading
across processes; not the steward's check alone. The acceptance is the steward's, and nothing of row a is built, filed or staged
until it is. The routing half keeps its own round.

**The decisions' notes.** D173's COWORKVIEW1 notes gain one dated 2026-10-25 for round 8, changing point 2 (an untaken
generation closed only at its own end or by its own process's absence, round 7's named changes' closure withdrawn; the token
held by the process that issued it, which every `start` names) and point 4 (the read woken as a token leaves and as another
issuer exits), and correcting round 7's paragraph where it promised more. D203's, D115's and D167's notes speak of the routing
half, which round 8 leaves as it was, so none gains a note; nor do D56's NOBLOCK1 note or D182. FOUNDATION1's §5.1 COWORKVIEW1 row
names round 8, a one-line edit of that design's row, as O7-F8 had it name round 7.

**What stays owed.** Both makers' reads of round 8, on row a's table (D191), and the steward's acceptance after them. *(Both
landed at `103fc1aa6`: Codex another round, 6 findings, 4 blocking, and the Opus foundation reader accept after named changes, 5
findings, 1 blocking, which §21 answers.)*

## 21. Round 9: both reads of round 8 answered

Both makers read round 8 at `103fc1aa6` (`docs/2026-10-24-cowork-view-reads.md`). Codex (*Codex, round 8*): 6 findings, 4 of
them blocking, on two points, a foreign issuer's death read as its agent's (its 1.1 and 2.2) and a competitor admitted late as a
go-on after a later run's taking (its 2.3), each also against D173's truthful partial visibility (its 3.1); *another round* for
row a. The Opus foundation reader (*Opus (foundation), round 8*): 5 findings, 1 blocking, its finding 1 the same late go-on;
*accept row a after named changes the steward can check without another read*. Every finding is checked against the code on
main at `103fc1aa6`, the commit both read and round 9 is written from. Each place round 9 changes is marked *(C8-n.m)*, Codex's
§n finding m, or *(O8-Fn)*, the Opus read's finding n. *Taken* means the design now says it, and where; *answered* means no
change is owed, with the reason. No finding is disputed: each held in the code it cites.

**The steward's direction for this round.** Each round had answered the last read with a new mechanism, and each read found a
new ordering in it. Round 9 adds none where the evidence is not there. Where a process's exit is not proven, its generation's
open children read in an honest uncertain state, `uncertain`, *not known whether it still runs*, never *lost* and never
*working*, and *lost* is drawn only where the exit is proven. The late go-on is taken as the Opus read names it, rule 2 by
takings in the file's order. The Opus read's findings 2 to 5 are taken where small, and where one would need more evidence the
uncertain state is preferred.

**Whether the cited lines moved.** Both reads cite `103fc1aa6`, the commit round 9 is written from, whose `src/` is `26ef34d80`'s
(`git diff --stat 26ef34d80 103fc1aa6 -- src` is empty), so no product line has moved since round 8, and their `Design:` lines
are this design's before round 9's edits. Every line they cite holds there, with three imprecisions in Codex's, none of which
changes its point. Its `SessionProcesses.cs:405` and `:665`, for the session-keyed replacement and removal, are `Track`'s
launcher test and the first line of `Untrack`'s `Dispose`; the replacement is `:408` and the removal `:668`, as the Opus read
cites them. Its `SessionLedger.cs:1724` is the brace after `:1723`'s test; the go-on is `:1725-1726`. Its foundation design
`:1266`, for FOUNDATION1 leaving row a independent, is §5.1's LIMITSWITCH1 row; the COWORKVIEW1 row is `:1270`, as the Opus read
cites it. Verified as both cite: `Platform/AgentJobs.cs:39-43`, `:46-49`, `:127`; `Platform/ProcessJob.cs:48-68`, `:50`, `:58`,
`:289`; `Sessions/SessionProcesses.cs:223`, `:403-409`, `:451-453`, `:500-508`, `:520-523`, `:527-530`, `:589`, `:623-625`,
`:640`, `:668`, `:675`; `Trees/Orphans.cs:74-81`; `Loop/Driver.cs:3673`, `:3810`, `:3831`, `:3834-3840`;
`Loop/DriverContinuations.cs:1242`; `Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1723-1726`, `:1821-1822`; `Modules/Loop/DriverLoop.cs:69`;
`Loop/LoopContext.cs:174`; `Platform/HomeLease.cs:426-449`; `fixtures/structure.json:126`, `:510`, `:576`; `D173.md:11-12`,
`:5747`. A passage round 9 changes cites `103fc1aa6`'s lines.

**What the check found beyond the reads.**
- **Containment is decided after the `start` is written, and kept on no record a reader holds.** `AgentStarts.StartAsync`
  contains a start as it makes it (`Platform/AgentStarts.cs:33`), after the `start` line, and the machine log's
  `agent.contained` line names no session (`:40-55`). So the steward's third proof, a contained process whose issuer is gone, is
  not one a reader of the file can tell. It would need a mark after the spawn, which §8 rejects, so such a process reads not
  known unless its record proves it.
- **A third way an agent outlives its issuer.** A start already the launcher's whose jobs do not hold this process's agents' job
  is uncontained (`AgentJobs.cs:111-117`), and `Track`, seeing the launcher, makes it no ORPHAN1 job (`SessionProcesses.cs:405-406`).
- **The lease's test is private and answers two ways** (`HomeLease.cs:427`, its *cannot tell* holding, `:444-448`). Named for
  row a, it is made one Platform function answering three, the lease's reading unchanged.
- **`AgentStart.Lives` already watches a process's exit** through a handle of its own (`AgentJobs.cs:260-283`), the shape the
  token set's exit watch follows, and `AgentJobs.Within` (`:203-208`) is the shape of the token set's test seam.
- **A refused run's process is ended by `AbandonedAsync`'s first line** (`Driver.cs:3971`), so the `end` written once its exit
  is read sits between that line and the capture's wait (`:3972-3990`), later by a kill's length.
- **A driven run writes its `end` on the ordinary path alone** (`Driver.cs:3910`, after its conclusion): a throw past it, a
  closing loop's among them, leaves its process to the reaper (`:3667`) and writes none. Round 8's token, leaving only once its
  `end` had been written or refused, would then have been held for good, its generation read *working* in its own window. Loop
  tells the token such a run's end, and it leaves at its process's exit (§3.6; rule 4).
- **Under round 9's reading, §9's window "reopened at its record's next run" draws not known, never *working*.** A generation the
  reading process issued is read by its token and never reopens, and one whose issuer went is closed by its record only while
  the record is past `working`.

**The verdict.** Codex asks another round on two points, both taken. No child reads *lost* where its process may still run, an
issuer's exit read as nothing of its agent, and the late go-on is read by takings in the file's order. The Opus read's blocking
finding is taken as named and its four others where it names them, the uncertain state taken in place of new evidence for its
finding 2's second half.

**The standing (D191).** Both makers' reads of round 8 have landed and are answered here. Whether row a needs another read, and
whose: D191 has Codex read every design and the Opus foundation reader read where a design changes a foundation, and a read may
accept after named changes the steward can check without another read. Round 9 is smaller than round 8. It adds no field and no
line kind, and it turns two of round 8's conclusions into a state that says it does not know; its one added placement is the
token's exit watch the Opus read asked for. The Opus read's finding 1 and the not-known state's words could be checked by the
steward against the code. But round 9 also changes how every recorded generation is read, a taken one by its token or issuer
rather than by `running` and its record, moves when a refused run's `end` is written, and places when a token leaves and where
its set lives. These are orderings between processes, the kind in which every read so far has found a hole its writer did not.
So this round judges that it is not a change the steward can check alone: both makers read round 9 before row a is accepted, as
the steward's direction for this round has it. What they need read is narrower than a whole round: §3.6's three ways and *how
a generation's process is read*, the token's life, rule 5's prefix cases and §9. The acceptance is the steward's, and nothing of
row a is built, filed or staged until it is. The routing half keeps its own round.

**The decisions' notes.** D173's COWORKVIEW1 notes gain one dated 2026-10-25 for round 9. It changes point 2 (a generation
closed by Daoris's reading only on proof of its process's exit, read by its issuer, and otherwise not known; rule 2 by takings;
every `end` after its process's exit; the token set one per Daoris process) and point 4 (the driver slice no input; the issuer
read by the lease's test), and corrects round 8's paragraph where it promised more. D203's, D115's and D167's notes speak of the
routing half, which round 9 leaves as it was, so none gains a note; nor do D56's NOBLOCK1 note or D182. FOUNDATION1's §5.1
COWORKVIEW1 row names round 9, a one-line edit of that design's row, as O7-F8 had it name round 7.

**What stays owed.** Both makers' reads of round 9, on row a's table (D191), and the steward's acceptance after them. The session
registry's defect §9 names is for its owner to file; this round files nothing.
